<page-num>83</page-num>
<header>Chariot of Antimony.</header>

### Chariot of Antimony.

Pour upon them good and perfectly rectified <term>Spirit of Vitriol</term> <note>original: "Spirit of Vitriol"; concentrated sulfuric acid</note>, and circulate the <term>mixture</term> until both are well conjoined and united. Then distill the whole, and pour on *Spirit of Wine* <note>original: "Spirit of Wine"; ethanol or high-proof alcohol</note>; circulate again. Then let a separation be made, and remove the <term>feces</term> <note>original: "Feces"; the dregs or solid residue left after distillation</note> settling to the bottom, but keep the <term>arcanum</term> <note>original: "Arcanum"; a secret, powerful remedy or "inner mystery" of a substance</note> which remains mixed with the *Spirit of Wine* and <term>vitriol</term>.

\* *This union must be so firm that, in distilling, one may not be separated from the other; otherwise, you will lose your labor and cost. Those best know how great a moment this union is in chemistry who think they shall effect wonders only by confounding two things together; but afterwards (their experience failing them) they learn how great a difference there is between union and confusion. For things merely confounded <note>original: "confounded"; mixed together without a chemical bond</note> receive no virtue <note>original: "Virtue"; medicinal power or inherent property</note> that was not in them before. But by union, I know not what spirit is ingested, which performs such things as the mind of man could never perceive to have their being from those sources. Hence, consider in the generation of animals (who would believe it!) how from the union of elements is generated sight, taste, touch, and so many powers of animals, which are inherent in none of the elements individually, and yet arise from them when united. Whenever it happens that any tincture seems to have united itself with its solvent <note>original: "Menstruum"</note>, and afterward may be separated from it, that is because the matrimony is not legitimately celebrated, nor the union perfected in a due manner; which you shall see happen more than once in the tincture of gold <note>original: "Sol"</note> and antimony.*

The instrument by which I conjoin my *tinctures*, and am accustomed to couple them in an undissolvable <term>copulation</term> <note>original: "Copulation"; a chemical bonding or joining</note>, you shall find described hereafter in this very book. Regarding the *Spirit of Wine*, it should be noted that it behaves differently than other *solvents*; because it is most easily united to things, and yet again separated from them with a slight artifice.

Now, when you again rectify this <term>arcanum</term>, one drop of it administered with *rose-water* is more effective than a pot full of the *decoction of herbs* <note>original: "Decoction of Herbs"; a herbal tea made by boiling plants</note>. For it causes a good appetite, corrects the stomach, and digests all malignity <note>original: "concocts all malignity"; processes or clears out harmful substances</note> within it. It drives away sadness and melancholy, creates good blood, and promotes good digestion. In the "suffocation of the womb" <note>original: "Suffocation of the Matrix"; a historical term for hysterical or respiratory distress attributed to the uterus</note> and the *colic passion* <note>original: "Cholick Passion"; severe abdominal pain</note> (both of which it wonderfully appeases), it is a treasure of inestimable price and deserves commendation not easily expressed by words.

### Basil Valentine's Triumphant Chariot of Antimony

After the Secret <note>original: "Arcanum"</note> of Antimony, next in order follows the Elixir of the same, which you may prepare in this manner:

<margin>This process is as easy to perform as it is for us to pass over quickly, lest we should seem to take pen in hand from a restless desire to write rather than a desire to illuminate obscure things.</margin>

Take, in the name of the Lord, good ore <note>original: "Minera"</note> of Antimony; grind it finely and sublime it with half as much Sal-Ammoniac <note>ammonium chloride</note>. Whatever has been sublimed, put into a glass retort and distill it three times, separating the dregs <note>original: "Feces"</note> every time. Afterward, remove the Sal-Ammoniac from it by washing <note>original: "edulcoration"</note>, and heat the matter of Antimony in a well-closed vessel with a moderate fire (not forcing it too much) until it becomes like the red earth of Cinnabar <note>mercury sulfide</note>.

This being done, pour on it strong distilled wine vinegar and extract its redness; afterward, evaporate <note>original: "abstract"</note> the vinegar until a powder remains. This evaporation must be done in a water bath <note>original: "Balneo"</note>. Then, extract this powder with spirit of wine <note>ethanol</note> so that the dregs may be separated, and you will have a pure and clear extraction. Having finished this operation, put this spirit of wine together with the extraction into a gourd-shaped chemical vessel <note>original: "Cucurbit"</note>, and add to it a little of the tincture of corals and of the quintessence of rhubarb; then administer a dose of three or four grains.

It causes gentle bowel movements and purges without stomach cramps <note>original: "gripings of the belly"</note>. Indeed, if you have proceeded well in the preparation, it makes the blood agile <note>original: "agil"</note> and is a medicine suitable for those who desire gentle laxatives <note>original: "purgations"</note>.

Here perhaps some physician may wonder how it is possible that this medicine should cause moderate and easy purging, seeing as Antimony is a matter...

### Chariot of Antimony.

The matter is vehement and forcible, and to it is joined <term>Rhubarb</term> <note>original: "Rubarb"</note>, which of itself also has a purging property. But let the reader cease his wonder and know that the venomous purging power of *<term>Antimony</term>* is so mortified <note>meaning: neutralized or subdued</note> by this preparation that it can seize or expel nothing on its own. However, as soon as some purging simple <note>a medicinal herb or substance consisting of only one ingredient</note> is joined to it, it then performs the office of opening and purging according to the powers of its own nature. Prepared *<term>Antimony</term>* has no action upon the *<term>stomach</term>* to expel its impurities by itself; but by the purging *<term>medicine</term>* added to it, it acquires a more open field. Therein it can operate without any other impediment and discern—even search out—the way of effecting its purpose more efficiently, as it was ordained and prepared to do without hindrance.

<margin>* Note here very seriously that <term>Galenic cathartics</term> <note>traditional herbal laxatives based on the theories of the Greek physician Galen</note> have the power of expelling, but not of correcting humors; but <term>chemical purgers</term> <note>medicines prepared through alchemy or early chemistry</note> are endowed with both faculties. Certainly, one should not focus on how much is expelled, but how much is healed, which the hidden power of <term>medicaments</term> prepared <term>chemically</term> effects much better than that common and public violence of purging forcibly.</margin>

I would have all men believe these words of mine, since I have no necessity to write anything other than the truth. This *<term>elixir</term>*, prepared in the manner I have taught, penetrates and purges the body just as *<term>antimony</term>* purges gold and frees it from all impurity. If I were to commemorate all its powers and virtues at length, I would have to offer my supplications to the *GOD* of Heaven and entreat Him to grant me a longer life, so that I might praise His wonderful works and search them out further. Then, I could truthfully communicate to others what I have found, so that they—moved to admiration along with me—might publicly render thanks to their *<term>Creator</term>* for such great blessings.

### Basil Valentine's Triumphant Chariot of Antimony

But to proceed in my purpose, having once begun, I here describe the virtues of *Antimony* <note>Antimony is a brittle, silvery-white metalloid; in this context, Valentine refers to its ores and chemical derivatives used in alchemy and medicine.</note> as far as I have experienced them. However, what is hidden from my knowledge, I ought to pass over in silence. For it does not become me to give my judgment of things unknown, and which I have not myself experimented with; instead, I leave them, commending the same to other judges who, through study and labor in this subject, have made some good progress. No one man can be so expert in knowing the virtues of *Antimony* that nothing shall remain unknown to him—not only by reason of the shortness of his life (as I said before) but also, and chiefly, because some new thing is daily found in it.

Therefore, let men know that *Antimony* not only purges gold, cleansing and freeing it from every foreign <note>original: "peregrine"</note> matter and from all other metals, but also (by a power innate in itself) effects the same in men and beasts. If a farmer intends to maintain and fatten any of his cattle—for example, a hog—let him give to the swine a convenient dose of crude *Antimony* (about half a dram mixed with its food) two or three days beforehand so that it may be purged by it. Through this purgation, the animal will not only acquire an appetite for its meat, but will increase in size and be fattened all the sooner. And if any swine suffers from a disease of its liver or other parts, or else is leprous <note>In historical veterinary contexts, "leprosy" often referred to various scaly skin diseases or mange.</note>, *Antimony* causes the leprosy to be dried up and expelled.

This example seems indeed to sound somewhat gross and rustic, especially to the ears of great men. However, my purpose in proposing it was only so that private men and laypeople <note>original: "Laicks"</note>—whose brains were not by nature built for the most subtle philosophy of the learned—may see the truth of this with their own eyes in the very operation itself. Also, I intended that by this simple <note>original: "rude"</note> proposal, they might give...

# The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

...give greater credit to my other writings, in which I have spoken a little more subtly of these things. Yet I would have no man, following me as his author, to give a medicine of crude <term>antimony</term> <note>original: "Crude Antimony"; raw, unprocessed stibnite ore</note> to men; for mute animals can concoct much harder foods in their stomachs than the tender constitution <note>original: "Complexion"</note> of men is able to digest. Therefore, he who would rightly and profitably use <term>antimony</term> must first learn its preparation, and afterward know the dose—specifically, what is appropriate for the young and what for the old; how much may be given to robust bodies and how much to the weak. In this knowledge consists no small mystery of this art, the ignorance of which will do more harm than an imprudent physician can do good.

Should I confirm all things by examples, it would result in a very long-winded <note>original: "Prolix"</note> writing; therefore, I will break off this discourse and pass on to another preparation of <term>antimony</term>, and describe its fixedness. This acts like wine from which the spirit has been drawn out <note>original: "substracted"</note> and separated from its body. This spirit heals the body internally, and if applied externally, it draws to itself all the heat of an inflamed part. 

But, on the contrary, when vinegar is made from wine, it cools whether it is applied inwardly or outwardly; although the wine and vinegar have their origin from the same root and proceed from the same stock. The reason for this diversity is because vinegar is made through digestion <note>in alchemy, "digestion" refers to a process of slow, gentle heating</note> only, by which the putrefaction of the wine follows, together with a vegetable fixation. On the contrary, spirit of wine is made with separation by distilling, or vegetable subliming, which renders the spirit volatile. By similar reasoning, <term>antimony</term> is prepared, and according to its various preparations, it has various effects and communicates its gifts to us in different ways, which are scarcely...

## Basil Valentine’s Triumphant Chariot of Antimony

<note>original: "Bafil Valentine's Triumphant"</note>

is scarcely comprehensible by the human intellect. But the fixedness of it, which I am treating here, is prepared in this way:

Take as much **Antimony** as you wish and grind it into a fine powder. Put this into a **cucurbit** <note>a gourd-shaped chemical vessel used for distillation</note> and pour enough **aqua fortis** <note>original: "Aqua fortis"; literally "strong water," a term for nitric acid</note> over it so that the liquid stands six fingers' breadth above the powder. Having closed the vessel well and firmly, place it in a gentle heat for ten days so that the essence of the matter may be extracted. Pour off this extraction while it is pure and clear, and filter it so that it is free from all dregs and impurities. 

Then, put this extraction into a glass body and draw off all the **aqua fortis** by distillation in ashes or sand. In the bottom, the powder of antimony will remain yellow and dry. Upon this, pour distilled rain-water and place it in a similar glass in moderate heat; you will then have a red extraction. Filter this again and gently distill off the rain-water using a **B.M.** <note>original: "B. M."; abbreviation for Balneum Mariae, or a "Mary's Bath" (a water bath/double boiler)</note>, and the powder will remain red at the bottom. 

Upon this red powder, pour strong distilled vinegar. In time, this vinegar will draw to itself a color as red as blood and leave behind the **feces** <note>original: "Feces"; the solid dregs or sediment remaining after a process</note>. Afterward, distill off the vinegar, and a red powder will remain once more. **Reverberate** <note>to heat in a furnace where the flames are reflected or "reverberated" onto the material</note> this powder continually for three days together without ceasing in an open fire. 

When this is done, extract the tincture from it using **spirit of wine** <note>original: "Spirit of Wine"; high-proof alcohol or ethanol</note>, and separate the remaining dregs from the tincture. After all these tasks have been performed with such great labor, again separate the spirit of wine by distillation in a water bath, and a fixed <term>Red Powder</term> <note>A "fixed" substance in alchemy is one that has been made stable and resistant to the effects of fire.</note> will remain, which operates wonderfully.

<note>Diaphoretic Antimony <note>a medicine used to induce sweating</note> is sold in shops; but what the author shows us here by the name of **Fixed Powder of Antimony** is not to be bought for silver or gold. Its virtues so far exceed all those of which the common sort are partakers, that it is in vain, even with great attention and study...</note>

# Chariot of Antimony.

(In preparing this powder, there is very great need for caution.) Our *Philosopher* <note>Likely referring to Basilius Valentinus, the purported author.</note> did not intend for this work, or these *Mysteries* of such great *Effects*, to fall into the hands of the *Vulgar* <note>original: "Vulgar"; meaning the uneducated or common people.</note> or be publicly sold for *Money*. Let him who attains this fixed *Powder* use it in *Chronic Diseases*, especially where *Sweats* are to be induced, and he will see *Effects* that will cause him to rejoice if he uses it on himself, and by which he will be glorified if he uses it on others.

Half a dram of this being taken thrice a day—namely *Morning, Noon, and Night*, or more often—hurts no man. For it expels all clotted *Blood* out of the *Body*, and when taken over a long period, it safely opens all dangerous *Abscesses* <note>original: "Imposthums"; a collection of pus or a swelling.</note> and expels them. It radically cures the *French Disease* <note>A common historical term for Syphilis.</note>, causes new *Hair* to grow, and notably renovates the whole man.

<margin>* How much *Chemistry* <note>original: "Chymistry"</note> was attacked by *Slanders* <note>original: "Calumnies"</note> in the times of *Basilius* is manifest by the many *Remnants* of *Writers*. Some *Theologians*, imprudently judging what they did not understand, and *Politicians* (not much more prudent than they) have defamed their own *Books* with these attacks. In the meantime, they have also given occasion to *Others*, who search more thoroughly into the matter, to judge that those very *Authors*—acting with no greater caution—have likewise splattered other innocent *Persons* with their *Censure*. I do not speak here of those writers who sharply rebuke certain *Vagabond Deceivers* <note>original: "Sophisters"</note> who cover their own *Wickedness* under the *Pretext* of a most noble *Art* and use a great Name to impose great *Frauds* upon the *People*. For this kind of men are not only worthy of severe *Rebuke*, but also of due *Punishment*. But what *Evil* do the practitioners deserve if the *Good* are abused under the same *Name* as the bad?</margin>

Now, since I have taught how to make a fixed *Powder* of *Antimony* and its *Extraction*, which is very convenient for *Use*, I will leave this *Discourse* and purpose briefly to treat of the *Flowers of Antimony* <note>A chemical term for the volatile sublimate or oxide of antimony produced by heating.</note>, which may be prepared in many ways. But the greatest part of men can neither discuss nor answer to these things because they have not learned the *Processes* of such *Operations*. However, the smaller part—namely the *Disciples, Apostles*, and *Followers* of the *Spagyric Art* <note>original: "Spagyrick Art"; an early term for Alchemical medicine, specifically the process of separating and recombining elements.</note>—will more highly esteem my *Writings*, more diligently read them, and more prudently give *Judgment* of the same.

# Basil Valentine's Triumphant Chariot of Antimony

Why is this most certain, healthy, and profitable Art <note>Alchemy/Chemistry</note> outlawed? It is because men are found who do not use the Art itself, but merely use its name and shadow. Yet I am unwilling to prolong this defense, lest envy—which has been the greatest cause of slander—fall upon me for lamenting in this way. Meanwhile, I hear Basilius <note>Basil Valentine</note> encouraging his followers, and I have reason to applaud our own times, in which some part of that thick cloud which drove out so many founders has already vanished. In shops today, they now sell medicines prepared chemically (as they say). Even those very persons who most wish to be esteemed as disciples of Hippocrates <note>Traditional physicians following ancient Greek medicine</note> scarcely dare to condemn chemistry; since they make use of things made by the chemical art, they cannot deny its value.

Are those times not at hand in which Elias the Artist, the revealer of greater mysteries, is to come? Paracelsus clearly prophesied his coming in various places in his writings. Perhaps it will be worth our while, for the comfort of the oppressed disciples of Basilius, to quote certain passages in which he predicts the coming of Elias, who was not then born. If anyone interprets these passages appropriately—as all other sayings of that man are to be taken—he will find nothing absurd in them, unless he chooses to reveal his own absurd stupidity or wicked envy. In the *Book of Minerals*, Chapter 8, Paracelsus writes:

> *What is most common, God allows to be discovered; but matters of greater importance are yet hidden from the common people until the coming of Elias the Artist.* 

Others read this as: *until the Art of Elias, when he comes.* And again, in his *Book of Minerals, Treatise the First*:

> *It is indeed true (he says) that many things lie hidden in the earth which I, as well as others, am ignorant of. For I know this: God, in time to come, will manifest His wonders and bring many more of them to light than have been known by us until this day. Also, this is true: there is nothing hidden which shall not be revealed; therefore, one is coming whose great work <note>original: "Magnale"; a Paracelsian term for a great talent or a miraculous work of God</note> does not yet live, who shall reveal many things.*

Therefore be comforted, be comforted, O lover of chemistry, and prepare the way for that Elias, who brings happy times and will reveal more secrets than our ancestors dared to discover because of envy and the wickedness of their days. Whosoever you are, practicing this Art, contribute some small matter toward this happiness. Let us give the world that medicine—which it cannot take all at once because of the dominant evil humors <note>In ancient medicine, "humors" were bodily fluids; here used metaphorically for the world's ill-will or corruption</note>—by degrees, so that it may gradually recover from its disease and the times of Elias may come. For Arts also (as is understood of other things) have their Elias, says Theophrastus <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, better known as Paracelsus</note>. In that time, it will be lawful for us to speak freely of these things and openly do good to our neighbors without the persecution of the ungodly. Read, understand, and comfort yourself with these thoughts.

> But, my disciple and follower, if you will imitate me, my advice is that you take up your cross at first and suffer as I have suffered. Learn to bear persecution as I have already borne it; labor, as all...

### The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

All our predecessors, along with me, have done the same: seek the Lord God with continual prayers. Be patient and hardworking without growing weary, and then the <term>Most High</term> <note>A title for God</note>, who has mercifully heard me, will not forsake you. For this cause, I give thanks to God every hour, as my eyes—filled with tears—can and do witness.

Now, to proceed in my instruction regarding the <term>Flowers of Antimony</term> <note>original: "Flores Antimonii"; these are the volatile sublimate or "smoke" of antimony that condenses into a powder</note>, it must be understood that the true Flowers of Antimony may be prepared in various ways, not just one, as is known to every <term>Spagyrist</term> <note>An alchemist who uses the "spagyric" method of separating and recombining elements to create medicines</note>. There are some who drive them over by a <term>Retort</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note> while mixed with <term>Sal-Ammoniac</term> <note>Ammonium chloride, used as a flux in alchemy</note>. Then they sweeten them by washing away the Sal-Ammoniac; they esteem these greatly, for these Flowers are of a beautiful white color. 

Others have specific instruments for this work, prepared with "windy caverns" <note>Air vents or flues</note> through which the antimony may receive air and be sublimed. Others, setting three <term>Alembic</term> <note>The cap or head of a still</note> vessels one upon another, sublime them with a strong fire; with one and the same labor, they produce white, yellow, and red Flowers. I have tried all these ways and found no error in them. However, the process by which I make Flowers of Antimony—which are most profitable for medicine and more effective in their operations—is this:

I mixed the red Flowers of Antimony with <term>Colcothar of Vitriol</term> <note>The brownish-red residue left after the distillation of sulfuric acid (vitriol)</note> and sublimed them together three times. In this way, the essence of the <term>Vitriol</term> <note>Sulfuric acid or iron sulfate</note> ascends with them, and the Flowers become stronger. Once this is done, I extract the same Flowers with <term>Spirit of Wine</term> <note>Ethanol or high-proof alcohol</note>. The <term>Dregs</term> <note>original: "Fæces"; the solid remains or sediment</note> settle to the bottom and separate themselves from the spirit. The craftsman sets these aside and distills off the Spirit of Wine in a <term>Bain-Marie</term> <note>original: "B. M."; a water bath used for gentle heating</note> until the powder remains dry.

These are my prepared Flowers of Antimony, which I administered to my brothers and others, who...

But regarding the sublimations of *Antimony*, which are afterward called *Flowers*, let the reader further observe this: they are like waters which break out in high mountains. Now, any man may judge the difference between waters that arise in the highest mountains—even higher than those, if such can be found, for the waters would ascend even to them, since it is known that springs often flow from the very tops of the highest mountains—and other waters found by digging deep into the earth and searching out their springs. 

One can see that the *Matrix* <note>The "womb" or place of origin where minerals and elements are formed</note> of waters in the earth is more abundantly replenished with the riches of water in one part than in another. This is because every element has a specific *Matrix*, either strong or weak, according to how it is produced by the *Star* <note>original: "Sydus"; referring to the astrological or celestial influence believed to govern the growth of minerals</note> from which the elements derive their origin and existence. Now, when such a *Matrix* is strong and potent in the earth, it can drive forth its seed strongly and effectively from itself by a vehement pressure,

<header>Chariot of Antimony.</header>

### The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

Even up to the snowy mountains of the Alps, or to the very top of the Tower of Babylon.

<margin>This parable is of very great importance, but it is so clear to those from whose eyes the cataract <note>A "cataract" here refers to a spiritual or intellectual blindness</note> has been removed, that to explain it to them would be like holding a candle to the light of the sun. To others, from whose sight the cataract has not been removed, this passage provides no opportunity to clear their vision. Therefore read, study, and meditate; "day is an instructor to the day" <note>original: *dies diei eructat verbum* - a reference to Psalm 19:2, meaning each day brings new wisdom</note>; the later reading explains the former, and the former explains the later.</margin>

If any foolish man hears this discourse of mine, he will say I am either mad or drunk with wine; but this will be nothing more than a reproach similar to that which was cast upon the disciples of our Savior <note>A reference to Acts 2:13, where the Apostles were accused of being drunk when speaking in tongues</note>. Regarding sublimation <note>The chemical process of heating a substance until it turns to vapor and then solidifies again, used here as a metaphor for spiritual purification</note>, the logic is the same: just as waters found in the highest mountains are often healthier and cooler than those found by digging deep into the earth in the plains, so it is with our work. If the matter is driven upward by the pressure and force of fire to the "mountains of the ancient wise men" (who died long before my time), the earth—which is mostly useless—remains behind until its salt <note>In alchemy, "salt" represents the non-combustible, solid essence of a substance</note> is extracted for its proper use. 

In this way, a separation is made, by which the evil may be distinguished from the good, the pure from the impure, the thick from the thin, the subtle from the coarse, and the venom from the medicine. We miserable mortals, because of the death we have inherited for our sins, are placed into the earth until we rot over time and are reduced to filth. Afterward, we are raised, clarified, and exalted by the heavenly fire and heat to a celestial sublimation. There, all our dregs <note>original: *feces* - used here in the archaic sense of dross, waste, or impure sediment</note>, all sins, and every impurity shall be separated from us, and we shall be made the sons of God and of eternal life—to which may the Lord, in His mercy and goodness, bring both me and you. I hope no man will take offense at this comparison of the worldly with the spiritual—namely, the heavenly exaltation with earthly sublimation.

<summary>The author uses the alchemical process of sublimation as a religious allegory for the purification of the soul and the resurrection of the body. He argues that just as fire separates medicine from venom, divine heat will separate human sin from the eternal spirit.</summary>

<keywords>sublimation, alchemy, spiritual purification, allegory, Tower of Babylon, salt, separation of impurities, resurrection</keywords>

# Basil Valentine's Triumphant Chariot of Antimony

limation. For no man should suppose that what I have done was done without reason; rather, he should know that I would be ignorant of the difference between white and black, or how much the dark and dismal air differs from the serene and clear heaven, if I acted otherwise. Therefore, let us conclude this discourse and present another preparation of <term>Antimony</term> <note>A metallic mineral used in alchemy and early medicine, often toxic if not prepared correctly.</note>.

Let the disciple, apostle, and faithful imitator of this Art understand this instruction. According to true philosophical doctrine, consider faithfully that every extraction from <term>Antimony</term> (as well as from all other things) has a great difference in its operation. This difference does not consist in the raw material itself, nor is it known by the material from which it is drawn; rather, it consists and remains in the preparation and in the addition <note>The solvent or chemical agent added to the base material.</note> by which the virtue is extracted from the matter, as is known by experience.

For example: whatever is extracted from <term>Antimony</term>, or any other thing, with <term>Spirit of Wine</term> <note>original: "Spirit of Wine"; high-proof alcohol or ethanol.</note> has a far different medicinal effect than those things which are extracted with good and sharp <term>Vinegar</term>. I have already hinted at the causes of this in my other writings; but the principal cause is because all extractions made with <term>Spirit of Wine</term>—whether from <term>Antimony</term> or any purging herbs, roots, or seeds (referring here to the first extraction)—powerfully expel by <term>Seidge</term> <note>original: "Seidge"; a bowel movement or stool.</note>. 

On the contrary, whatever is extracted with <term>Vinegar</term> does not purge downward, but rather prevents that. It retains the substance in a certain peculiar manner, by which the <term>Volatile</term> <note>Substances that easily evaporate or are chemically active.</note> is changed into the <term>fixed</term> <note>Substances that are stable and resistant to change by heat.</note>.

This is a singular secret and an <term>Arcanum</term> <note>A deep mystery or powerful chemical secret.</note> of great price. However, there are none, or very few, to be found who pay attention to such things. Many things are hidden and lie deeply buried in this <term>Arcanum</term> which no one—neither master nor servant, nor any wise man of this world—has observed or considered until this day.

<summary>Basil Valentine explains that the medicinal properties of Antimony depend entirely on the solvent used, noting that alcohol-based extracts act as laxatives while vinegar-based extracts fix the substance.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the chemical preparation of Antimony and its medicinal effects, specifically focusing on the differences between extractions made with vinegar versus spirit of wine.</meta>

<page-num>95</page-num>
<header>Chariot of Antimony.</header>

# Chariot of Antimony.

Therefore, the extraction of *Antimony* is performed in two ways: namely, with vinegar and with spirit of wine. Vinegar obstructs <note>original: "obstructs," meaning it inhibits or alters the purging effect</note>, while spirit of wine by itself powerfully expels and causes urination, as well as bowel movements. This is shown elsewhere in another manner, especially where I speak of my *Twelve Keys* <note>A reference to the author's famous alchemical work, *The Twelve Keys of Basil Valentine*</note>: namely, that the extraction of glass of *Antimony* <note>term: Glass of Antimony; a transparent, reddish-orange substance created by roasting and melting antimony ore</note> purges moderately. But on the contrary, the extraction of the same glass which is made with vinegar does not purge; this is true and not mixed with any falsehood. 

This experience provides an opportunity for thought and reflection; by this way of reasoning, Nature promotes and produces a true philosopher. It is a thing much to be admired that every subject which is first of all extracted with spirit of wine should have a purging property. Yet, when glass of *Antimony* is extracted from the beginning with distilled vinegar, and that vinegar is then removed <note>original: "abstracted"</note>, and the *Antimony* is then extracted with spirit of wine, all its venomous purging virtue passes away. No sign of it remains, nor does it take upon itself any power of provoking bowel movements. Instead, it performs its operations through sweat and other ways, chiefly by salivation and ejection by the mouth. It searches out all harmful evils in the body, purges the blood, heals diseases of the lungs, and benefits those who are short of breath <note>original: "strait-breasted"</note> and troubled with a frequent cough. In a word, it cures very many diseases, also eases a malignant cough and whatever is of that nature, and is a very admirable medicine.

Moreover, there is another extraction of *Antimony* made in this manner: Grind crude *Antimony* to powder and pour upon it strong vinegar—not made of wine, but made of its own proper mineral <note>original: "minera"; likely referring to a vinegar or acid derived from the same mineral source</note>—and expose the mixture in a well-closed vessel to the solar <note>The text continues on the next page with "Solar heat" or "Solar rays"</note>

<summary>This page explains how different solvents, specifically vinegar and spirit of wine, radically change the medicinal properties of Antimony, turning it from a purgative into a sweat-inducing remedy for lung ailments. It emphasizes the alchemical principle that the method of extraction determines whether the substance acts as a poison or a cure.</summary>

<margin>Here your sincerity, Valentine, is suspected. What shall I say? Malice makes you not a wicked man, but a fearful one, fearing that if these secrets <note>original: "Arcanums"</note> were communicated openly to the wicked, they would do more harm with them than good. This process seems to me to be purposely described obscurely by this religious man (why else would he not have written more clearly?) because he knew the "Sons of Art" <note>A traditional term for dedicated alchemists</note> would understand it by his instructions. But so that you, Reader, may feel your money and time were well spent on these commentaries, I will clearly and openly reveal to you how I myself have often performed this very work. Pay close attention to everything so that you are not lacking in any detail; for the operations are so linked together that if one is omitted or performed negligently, all the others are spoiled, and your labor will not fulfill your desires.</margin>

Put it into a cucurbit <note>A gourd-shaped chemical vessel</note> and pour upon it vinegar which, according to the art, is distilled from its own proper ore. # The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

Place this double vessel in a *Bain-Marie* <note>original: "B. M."; a heated water bath used for gentle, uniform heating</note> to be digested until you see the *Tincture* <note>A concentrated liquid extract</note> raise itself upward and pass over as a volatile substance through the *Alembic* <note>An early chemical still consisting of two vessels connected by a tube</note>. Seeing this, cool your vessel and put all the *matter* into a *cucurbit* <note>A gourd-shaped chemical vessel used for distillation</note>, and distill the *spirit* according to the *art* <note>The "Art" refers to the skilled practice of Alchemy/Chemistry</note>, which will pass over the *helm* <note>The top or "head" of the still</note> as red as *blood*. Then abstract the *spirit* again, and you will have a most heavy, thick *oil*.

How this *oil* should afterward be joined and united with its own *salt*, we speak of elsewhere; its use in improving *metals* is very great. Here we have described it and brought it to the point where it becomes a most excellent *medicine* for grievous *diseases* which are everywhere accounted incurable. It performs its entire *operation* by inducing *sweats*. And since there is no *disease* which it does not help, what need is there to name any? It is better to be altogether *silent* than to detract from its *praises* by reciting only a few. There is no man who has made any *progress* in *chemistry* who does not know what the *quintessence* <note>The purest or most concentrated essence of a substance</note> of *antimony* is.

The physician, before all things, should diligently contemplate its use according to his own experience and knowledge, especially completing its preparation in a proper manner without being weary of his labor or forgetting any of those things which are prescribed to be performed.

Again, another extraction of *antimony* is established thus: Take, in the name of the Lord, equal parts of *antimony* and crude *tartar* <note>Potassium bitartrate, a sediment from wine making</note>. Put these powders, well mixed together, into a strong *crucible* which will not allow the *spirits* to penetrate it, and burn this *matter* in the *fire* until the *tartar* is wholly consumed; this work must be done in a *wind-furnace* <note>A furnace that uses a draft of air to reach high temperatures</note>. Grind this burnt *matter* to a fine *powder* and pour on common *water* that has first been made hot, and so wash the *matter* by *lixiviating* <note>The process of leaching or washing a solid to extract a soluble substance</note>. And so it becomes a *liver* <note>original: "Hepar"; a chemical term for a liver-colored sulfur compound</note>, so called by many of our *ancestors* who lived long before us.

<margin>The *liver* is a cause of much *discourse* in *banquets*, and the *liver* of *antimony* will be to us no small occasion for the same in *chemical operations*.</margin>

But in the first place, consider that instead of *crude tartar*, it is better to take *salt of tartar* <note>Potassium carbonate</note>, not only for *accelerating* the *operation* (for otherwise it must be...

Otherwise, because of the Salt of Tartar, as soon as the Glass hits the cold air, it changes into a kind of pulse or pap <note>a soft, semi-liquid mash</note>. Besides, he omitted to specify the quantity of water in which this powder should be dissolved so that we may obtain a more beautiful and deep color; in which "exaltation" <term>the process of intensifying or refining a substance's properties</term> of color, much is achieved. *Also, the precipitation of the Liver from the water*, which must be done with vinegar, was shrouded in silence; although this, and the steps mentioned above, are necessary in this operation.

*Here I am unwilling to refrain from advising* that not only in this, but in every melting of Antimony, the artist <note>the alchemist or chemist</note> ought to observe the "Face of Heaven" <note>the astrological alignment of the planets and stars</note> and begin his work when the sky is clear and serene. If you do otherwise, you will later in vain regret the dullness of the color in your Liver of Antimony. For if you laugh at me for attributing much virtue to the influences of the stars, I shall mock you when you are lamenting the unlucky success of your many processes. But I would not digress so far as to take it upon myself to prove the power of celestial influences against men who either know so little that they cannot understand, or who, having experienced some results, fail to perceive the cause. In the meantime, they are so obstinate that they will not believe the things they see, have tested, and have indeed found to be true. *I would no more urge reasons against these men* than against those who deny even the transmutation <note>original: "Mutation"</note> of metals by the Philosopher's Stone. 

We judge such men more worthy of contempt than refutation—those who say that what they have beheld with their eyes and done with their hands is impossible. For it is very ignorant to simply say, "I deny it, I deny it, who can prove it?" These perverse men, by acting this way, sometimes provoke the patience of good and ingenious artists, thinking that the artists, out of a zeal to prove their claims, will go so far as to reveal the secrets of such an excellent art to them. But I do not think that anyone who has obtained the "Mystery" <note>the secret knowledge of alchemy</term> will be so imprudent as to thoughtlessly "cast pearls before swine" and discover to the unworthy what God has revealed to him; or give them the bread he eats, which is not to be cast to dogs.

*Therefore, returning to the matter from which I digressed*, I affirm that there are some times of the year in which, if Liver of Antimony is made and precipitated with a proper solvent <note>original: "Menstruum"</note>, it will be imbued with an entirely different color and endowed with different virtues than that which is made at another time. This applies both to the work with metals and to the healing of human infirmities, as I myself have experienced in many grievous diseases and symptoms. I will also add this: from the Liver of Antimony, a redness may be extracted with "Spirit of Wine" <note>ethanol or high-proof alcohol</note>. This redness is made volatile and passes over the "helm" <note>the top part of a still or alembic</note>, and it may be administered to anyone without danger. Nor is there any great need for that caution of Basilius—even before the redness is made volatile—that it must be given in a small quantity. For when given in doses of thirty to forty or fifty drops, it causes a very moderate sweat and does not cause purging or vomiting.

But it effects wonders in purging the blood, uprooting the causes of a disease, and making the patient vigorous through insensible perspiration <note>original: "insensible Expiration"; the release of toxins through the skin without visible sweating</note> and its hidden <note>original: "occult"</note> virtues. This marketable wine needs no "bush of eloquence" <note>A reference to the proverb "Good wine needs no bush," meaning a good product needs no advertisement.</note> to commend it to the sick; those who have once experienced its virtues know what it can do, and that it satisfies their desires as much as they value their money.

Put this dried "Liver of Antimony" <note>A glass-like substance formed by fusing antimony with potassium nitrate.</note> into a cucurbit <note>A gourd-shaped chemical vessel used in distillation.</note> and pour upon it the most pure spirit of wine <note>Ethanol or high-proof alcohol.</note>. What happens in this operation is very admirable. For the spirit of wine that was drawn off can no longer be united with the red extraction from which it was distilled; instead, one floats above the other like water and oil, which cannot be mixed. But if that same distilled spirit of wine is poured upon *other* Liver of Antimony, it again attracts the color to itself; yet even then, if poured upon the former extraction, it cannot be mixed with it. This is a thing so strange that it may be numbered among wonders. But who can declare all the wonders of **GOD**? Or who will worthily value the gifts of the Creator which He has implanted in His creatures? These things are scarcely searchable by us, even with deep meditation.

I previously mentioned the extraction of Antimony from its glass <note>Glass of Antimony: a transparent, reddish-yellow glass produced by roasting and melting antimony ore.</note> by vinegar, and also by spirit of wine. But now I say: when such an extraction is made by vinegar, and the vinegar is again drawn off by a water bath, and the powder which remains is dissolved <note>original: "resolved"</note> in a moist place into an oil or liquor of a yellow color, it effects such wonders in wounds—both new and old—that I neither can, nor dare, commit them all to writing.

# The Method of Distillation

<image-desc>A diagram illustrating a chemical apparatus, likely for distillation or condensation. It features a large, rectangular chamber with a domed top, labeled 'A', and two square openings on its front face. A spigot or outlet is labeled 'B'. This chamber is connected by a curved tube to a smaller, round flask, labeled 'C'. Flask 'C' is connected by another tube to a slightly larger, round flask, labeled 'D'. Finally, flask 'D' is connected by a tube to the largest, round flask, labeled 'E', positioned at the bottom left.</image-desc>

<margin>Conflict in this</margin>
<margin>all parts; which brings forth a matter</margin>

As you can see in the figure above, the vessel marked **A** serves as the primary furnace or heating chamber. From this vessel, the vapors are drawn through the connecting tubes into the series of receivers marked **C**, **D**, and **E**. This arrangement allows for the gradual cooling and collection of the spirits <note>original: "spirits"; in this context, refers to the volatile essence or concentrated liquid produced by distillation</note>. 

You must ensure that the joints are sealed tightly so that no essence escapes during the process. If the heat is managed with reason and careful consideration, you will overcome the natural dullness of the raw material. Many who are ignorant of this understanding fail to achieve the desired profit, which is the restoration of health and the advancement of chemical knowledge. 

By following this preparation, which I have titled and described at length in my other writings, you will bring the hidden virtues of the substance to light. It is not an extraordinary feat achieved by spirits <note>original: "by spirits"; here meaning supernatural entities</note>, but rather by the diligent administration of the fire and the proper use of the apparatus.

<summary>This page describes a specific chemical distillation apparatus, illustrating how vapors travel through a series of receivers to be condensed into medicinal or chemical spirits. The author emphasizes the importance of proper technique and understanding over superstitious explanations for chemical reactions.</summary>

--- Page 134 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical description of chemical distillation processes, specifically focusing on the assembly of a multi-vessel apparatus for capturing volatile substances.</meta>

<page-num>101</page-num>

### The Description of the Distilling Apparatus

The illustration above represents a sophisticated system for the distillation and collection of chemical spirits. 

**A** marks the primary vessel, or the body of the furnace <note>original: "athanor" or "body"</note>, where the initial substance is heated. The spout, labeled **B**, serves as the primary outlet for the rising vapors. 

As the vapors exit the first vessel, they pass through a series of interconnected receivers:

*   **C** and **D** represent the first large receiving flask, divided to allow for the initial cooling and condensation of the heavier spirits.
*   **E** is the secondary receiver, designed to catch the more volatile or "subtle" vapors that did not condense in the first stage.
*   **F** is the final flask in the sequence, acting as a safety receiver to ensure that no precious medicinal or chemical essence is lost to the open air.

<margin>The connection of many vessels allows for the purification of the spirit in a single process.</margin>

This arrangement is particularly useful for the distillation of mineral acids or essential oils, where the vapor must be cooled gradually across several stages to prevent the glass from breaking due to intense heat, and to ensure the highest purity of the final product. The tubes connecting each flask must be sealed tightly with "lute" <note>A chemically resistant paste or cement used to seal joints in lab equipment</note> to prevent the escape of any fumes.

<summary>This page provides a technical diagram and description of a multi-stage distillation apparatus used to capture and refine volatile chemical spirits through a series of glass receivers.</summary>
<keywords>distillation, chemical apparatus, retort, receiver, condensation, laboratory equipment, alchemy</keywords>

## The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

...a matter like thin butter, or the sediment of olive oil; keep this apart for use.

* Here I will teach you the manual operation, O lover of <term>chemistry</term> <note>original: "Chymistry"</note>, which you will undoubtedly value greatly when you discover its great convenience in practice. Open the upper hole (B) of your earthen <term>retort</term> <note>a glass or clay vessel with a long downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</note> (A). Put your matter into it in small portions, rather than all at once, so that the heat does not cause it to react too violently; then immediately close the hole with its proper cover. The spirits received in the vessel (C) are given an exit through the hole (D) into the opposite receiver (E), to which another receiver (F) is also attached. In this way, the more subtle spirits ascending through the hole (D) settle in the recipient (F), while the coarser spirits remain in the bottom (G) of the receiver (C). This instrument will be most suitable for your use here—not only, as I said, to prevent a strong spirit from breaking the glass, but also for other works, as you will gather from simple observation.

Finely grind the <term>caput mortuum</term> <note>Latin: "dead head"; the useless solid residue remaining after distillation</note> and allow it to dissolve into water in a cellar. Keep this water and filter it through paper. Afterward, take Hungarian <term>antimony</term>, grind it to a fine powder, and place it in a <term>cucurbit</term> <note>a gourd-shaped chemical vessel used for boiling or distilling</note> with a flat bottom. Pour this water over it and place the vessel in heat. When it has stood there for a sufficient time, the matter will look like an amethyst, with a blackish-violet color. Then increase the fire significantly, and you will obtain a transparent color like a blue sapphire. 

From this colored liquid, precipitate a white powder by pouring in common water. This powder, when taken, has the same effect as the red extraction of glass of antimony made by <term>seidge</term> <note>likely a reference to a specific decoction or boiling process</note>, and it also induces vomiting. <margin>Chemists—in order to sharpen the wits of their readers and to ensure that no one but true students of the Art should penetrate their secrets—do not deliver all information in one place. Instead, they scatter their instructions so that by diligently collecting them, they may judge a student's fitness, just as the eagle tests her young by exposing them to the sun's rays. Thus, you see Basil here proposes the use of the "Water of Mercury," which he taught how to make in his treatise called the *Supplement*, or rather, he gave a rough draft of that process there. For neither there nor here does he mention that a hole must be left open in the upper part of the retort <note>a glass vessel with a long neck used for distillation</note>, into which a long pipe is fitted, through which the mercury may be added in very small amounts. For if you enclose even a small amount of mercury in a retort that has first been made very hot with a violent fire—as this must be if you wish to extract the spirit—that mercury, with its own violent and uncontrollable force, would not only burst the retort but also overthrow the furnace itself, unless you give it a larger space and greater liberty to fly; so that, after it has passed through many retorts, it may rest and, being as it were tired, settle. But since it is well known to all true chemists how great the power of this mercury is in the dissolving of metals, I will make no further mention of it here.</note></margin>

Take one part of this extraction; three parts of the Red Extraction of Antimony (which is prepared with fixed saltpeter and spirit of wine <note>alcohol</note>); one part of the Water of Mercury, added slowly through a long pipe;

# Chariot of Antimony

<margin>The process, heat, and preparation will be explained shortly.</margin>
In the improvement of metals, which *Basilius* <note>Basilius Valentinus, the purported 15th-century alchemist and author of this work.</note> does not so much as mention. Now the genius of all *chemists* will understand me, as I am here candidly discovering this secret to all. Do you, lover of *chemistry*, in mind and thought swiftly follow me as I expound the *oracle* <note>The "oracle" refers to the hidden chemical truth or the cryptic instructions of the art.</note>; however, the actual operation cannot be swiftly performed.

<margin>Mercury and some wine, carefully handled.</margin>
This powder must first be extracted with strong *vinegar*, which you must afterward draw off <note>original: "abstract"</note>. What remains in the bottom, wash until sweet <note>term: "edulcorate" — to wash a solid to remove soluble salts or acids</note> with *distilled water*. Again, extract it with *spirit of wine* <note>term: "spirit of wine" — ethanol or high-proof alcohol</note>, and draw off the *spirit*; in the bottom will remain a red powder. Join this with the fixed *salt*, which is made of the *dregs* <note>original: "fæces"</note> that remained after the *vinegar* was used for *extraction*. Deliver it to *Vulcan* <note>note: Vulcan was the Roman god of fire; here it refers to the alchemical furnace.</note> for the space of three months, so that it may no longer fly from the *fire*, but most pleasingly sport with and in the same. If you perform this, you have two conjoined in an inseparable *matrimony*: you have separated the pure from the impure, have rendered the *volatile* fixed, and fixed the *volatile*, and are not far from that felicity which will answer all your desires.

<margin>A caustic mixture and the dissolution.</margin>
The *dead head* <note>term: "caput mortuum" — the useless residue left after distillation or sublimation</note> which is left, being dissolved in a humid place, yields a liquid so sharp that no *nitric acid* <note>original: "aqua fortis"</note> may be compared with it in *sharpness*. But of these things, enough has been said at this time. For I must now speak of a white powder, which may also be prepared from *antimony* in this manner.

<margin>The process.</margin>
Take pure *antimony*, which is brought from *Hungary* <note>original: "Hungaria"</note> or found in similar mountainous places; grind it to a subtle powder. Take also the same measure of pure *saltpeter* <note>original: "salnitre"</note>, which has been diligently cleansed three times. Burn this *composition* in a new glazed pot (which was never contaminated with any *fatness*) in a *circulatory fire*; do not do this all at once, but by parts and at various times. This way of operating the ancient *alchemists* <note>original: "spagyrists"</note> called *detonation*, a term of art to be learned by the disciple of the art, as it is not common to every peasant <note>original: "rustick"</note> in his craft and experience.

<margin>The extraction.</margin>
This operation being performed, grind the hard matter which remained in the pot to a fine powder. Upon it, in another glazed pot, pour warm common water. When the matter has settled, repeat the pouring on of water several times until all the *saltpeter* is extracted.

Whatever in the body is corrupted and declined from its healthy state, it amends and restores. 

What wonderful effects this single **tincture** has revealed to me, I forbear to mention, lest I should be compelled to bring the reputation of those who have experienced them into question. In this only believe me, whoever you are that would use **chemical medicines**: always be sure to take the true **tinctures** of things, in which their volatile **sulfur** is hidden; if you neglect this, you neglect your own reputation and profit, and the health of the sick.

Equity <note>original: "Equity"; here meaning fairness or natural justice</note> seems to require this, and yet it cannot be described or declared by me. <margin>Valentine has so clearly revealed all things that no man, either before or after him, has done so more plainly. All who came after him seem to have conspired and agreed together to spread clouds over that light which he brought into the world. Hence it is that they do not publicly extol his praises according to the high esteem every man privately has for him; nor have they translated his books into other tongues, although he, of all authors, is the most worthy to speak in the languages of all nations. But no man should think that he could speak so clearly that every man handling chemistry with "unwashed hands" (as the saying goes) might immediately understand him. That is, as he himself says, not possible to do, nor is it appropriate that the son of a lowly peasant should eat of the finest flour in the preparation of which he took no pains. Yet, as he confesses below, our author has used plain, simple, and clear words.</margin>

But I make too large a progress in this open field of doctrine, in which the ancient hunters take their larks, and the young ones immediately follow them with their nets. For my style (as all my writings witness) has a certain singular, intentional method, like that of all philosophers before me. If anyone thinks it strange that I here propose certain unique processes in which my philosophy differs from others, let him be answered with this: philosophical speech differs greatly from the method of other men's discourses, who nakedly and sincerely declare some process without any ambiguities or the clouding of *enigmas* <note>Riddles or obscure metaphors used to hide alchemical secrets from the unworthy</note>. ### Chariot of Antimony.

It may seem as if I had deviated from the proper order in my style of philosophy, and in my descriptions of preparations and processes. However, in a philosophical discourse, it is necessary to learn and judge what pertains to the theory; but it is practice that teaches you the instruction of processes. Therefore, in describing them, true, simple, clear, and well-grounded words are to be used.

Also, from **Antimony** <note>original: "Antimony"; a metallic element often used in alchemy and early medicine</note> is made a **Balsam**, which is very profitable against grievous diseases. Yet this is not made of **Crude Antimony**, but of its **Regulus** <note>original: "Regulus"; the pure metallic heart of the mineral, separated from the slag</note>, from which may be made living **Mercury** in the following manner:

Take equal parts of the best **Hungarian Antimony** and crude **Tartar** <note>original: "Tartar"; potassium bitartrate, a sediment from wine casks</note>, and half a part of **Saltpeter** <note>original: "Salnitre"</note>. Grind them well together, and afterward melt <note>original: "flux"</note> them in a **Wind-Furnace**. Pour out the flowing matter into a **Cone** <note>original: "Cone"; a conical mold used to separate the metal from the dross</note>, and there let it cool. Then you will find the **Regulus**, which you must purge by fire three times or more with **Tartar** and **Nitre** <note>saltpeter</note>. It will then be bright and white, shining like **Refined Silver** <note>original: "Cupellate Silver"</note> that has been purified and has overcome all its lead.

<margin>What I advised regarding the preparation of **Liver of Antimony** <note>a sulfurated oxide of antimony</note> is to be repeated here: instead of crude **Tartar**, take **Salt of Tartar**, by which the operation will proceed sooner and better. **Saltpeter** here is unprofitable. Therefore, take equal parts of **Antimony** and **Salt of Tartar**, melt them, and make a **Regulus** according to the rule given here by **Basilius** <note>referring to Basil Valentine, the purported author</note>. If you cast away the **Glass** <note>the scoria or slag left over from the melting</note>—as most men do—you will do poorly. For I prepare a very profitable **Medicine** <note>original: "Medicament"</note> from that glass in this manner: I grind this glass in a hot **Mortar**, taking care that it absorbs no moisture <note>original: "Humidity"</note> from the air (which may easily be prevented). Having put it in a **Vial** <note>original: "Phial"</note>, I pour **Alcohol of Wine** <note>highly distilled ethanol</note> upon it, and from there extract a most beautiful **Tincture**, red in color like **Blood**. This tincture is a most excellent **Cordial** <note>a medicine intended to stimulate the heart and spirits</note> if thirty, forty, or fifty drops of it are taken in a suitable liquid, twice or thrice a day if you wish; for it is taken with safety and restores the whole man.</margin>

Also, many oils may be prepared from *antimony*—some *by itself* <note>original: per se</note> and without addition, and many others by adding other substances. Yet they are not all endowed with the same virtues; rather, each possesses its own specific properties according to the diversity of its preparation. 

I will now give you a comparison for this. There are many kinds of animals that live only in the earth, such as many creeping things, worms, and serpents. There are also others—some of which are new kinds that did not exist before—which also arise from the putrefaction <note>the natural decomposition of organic matter, believed at the time to spontaneously generate life</note> of the earth. Some inhabit the waters, such as all kinds of fish; others fly through the air, such as every kind of winged creature and bird; some are even nourished in the fire, such as the salamander <note>a mythical creature believed in the 16th century to live in or endure fire</note>. 

Furthermore, in the hotter regions and islands, many other animals are found that are unknown to these nations; these prolong their lives by the solar heat and die as soon as they are brought into a different climate. In the same way, when *antimony* is prepared by the addition of water, it assumes a different nature and temperament for its medicinal operation than when it is prepared by fire alone. And although every preparation of it must be made by fire—without which its inner virtue cannot...

It may not be immediately obvious, but consider that the addition of Earth gives a substance an entirely different nature than the addition of Water. Similarly, when **Antimony** is sublimed <note>sublimed: heated until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again</note> in Fire through the Air and further prepared, a different virtue, different powers, and a different operation follow than in the preparations already described. Therefore, the Oil of **Antimony** by itself, without any additions, and its true Sulphur are prepared according to this method:

Take crude **Hungarian Antimony** and grind it into a fine powder. Place it into a glass **cucurbit** <note>cucurbit: a gourd-shaped chemical vessel used in distillation</note> with a flat bottom, and pour over it the true **Vinegar of the Philosophers** <note>Vinegar of the Philosophers: a specialized chemical solvent, often mercury-based or a highly rectified spirit of vinegar</note> which has been made more acidic with its own salt. Then, place the cucurbit, firmly sealed, in horse dung or a **B.M.** <note>original: Balneum Mariae; a heated water bath</note> to let the matter putrefy for forty days. During this time, the "body" of the mineral dissolves, and the vinegar takes on a color as red as blood.

Pour off this vinegar and pour on fresh vinegar; repeat this process until the vinegar can no longer be tinted red. Once this is finished, filter all the vinegar through paper. Place it into a clean glass, firmly sealed, and set it again in horse dung or a water bath as before, so that it may putrefy for another forty days. During this time, the body resolves itself again, and the matter in the glass becomes as black as **calcanthum** <note>calcanthum: vitriol or copperas, used in making ink</note> or shoemaker's ink.

When you see this sign, a true solution has been achieved, by which the further separation of the elements is made possible. Put this black matter into another cucurbit, attach an **alembic** <note>alembic: a cap or "head" used to conduct vapors into a cooling tube for distillation</note>, and distill off the vinegar with a moderate fire. The vinegar will come out clear, and a foul-looking matter will remain at the bottom. Grind that residue into a fine powder and **edulcorate** <note>edulcorate: to purify or sweeten by washing away salts and acids</note> it with distilled rain water. Then, dry it with gentle heat and place it in a **circulatory** <note>circulatory: a vessel used for continuous distillation where the vapor condenses and flows back into the base</note> with a long neck. The circulatory vessel must have three cavities or "bellies," as if three globes were stacked one above another, yet...

## Basil Valentine's Triumphant

The vessels should be distinct or separate from each other, just as sublimatories <note>Apparatus used to heat a solid until it turns into vapor and then collects as a solid again.</note> with their aludel <note>original: "Aludel [or Head]"; a pear-shaped pot open at both ends used as a condenser in the sublimation process.</note> are usually made. Having well closed the vessel, set it in a moderate heat for two months. 

Then follows another new *extraction*, and the spirit of wine becomes transparently red like a ruby; or like the first extraction made with vinegar, but even more beautiful. Pour off the spirit of wine that has been thus tinted, filter it through paper, and put it into a cucurbit <note>A gourd-shaped chemical vessel used in distillation.</note>. (Set aside the black matter which remains and separate it from this work, for it is of no use here.) To the cucurbit, attach a head <note>The top part of a still.</note> and a receiver, and having firmly sealed all the joints, begin to distil in a bed of ashes with a moderate fire. 

Then the spirit of wine carries the tincture of antimony over with it; the elements separate from each other, and the alembic <note>The distilling apparatus.</note> and the recipient vessel seem to resemble pure, transparent gold in appearance. In the end, a few dregs <note>original: "Feces"</note> remain, and the golden color in the glass completely disappears. 

Put the red matter, which passed over into the receiver during distillation, into a circulatory vessel <note>A vessel where vapors condense and flow back into the base to be re-distilled continuously.</note> for ten days and ten nights. By that circulation, a separation occurs; for the oil thereby acquires weight <note>original: "Gravity"</note> and separates itself, sinking to the bottom beneath the spirit of wine. The spirit of wine becomes clear again, just as it was at first, and floats upon the oil. This wonderful separation is like a miracle in nature. Separate this oil * from the spirit of wine using a separatory funnel.

## The Triumphant Chariot of Antimony

The books of all chemists <note>original: "Chymists"</note> are filled with this subject, which is involved in so many fables, complicated by so many riddles, and explained with so many obscure commentaries. It is that which is despised by fools throughout the world, sought by the "Sons of Art" <note>A traditional term for dedicated alchemists</note>, and found by the wise. This, Basilius reveals; this, he repeats; this, he emphasizes. This is his *Triumphant Chariot*, which he carries about, as it were, and often shows in various parts of his writings.

Earlier in this treatise, he presents it under the name of an "Extraction of Crude Antimony"; here, it is "Oil of Antimony"; soon after, it is converted into a stone, which is called the "Stone of Fire." Thus, this Proteus <note>A Greek sea-god capable of changing his shape; used here to describe how antimony changes its form while remaining the same substance</note> often offers itself—always various, yet always the same in substance. Compare all these processes, which are so often proposed in different ways, with these commentaries of mine made upon the extraction of crude antimony, and you will have the work complete in all its parts. You will have a treasure in which, if you do not recognize what you have, I refer you to Aesop’s rooster on the dung-hill, who found a gem in the manure but did not know its value.

Consider diligently, O lover of chemistry, and you will find that no man has dealt more clearly and sincerely with you than Basilius—and me after him—who show you where the "hare lies" <note>A metaphor for finding the hidden truth or the heart of the matter</note> which so many others have hunted in vain. If you are not wise now, you will not be healed even by three Anticyras <note>original: "Anticyra's"; a reference to ancient Greek cities famous for producing hellebore, a plant used to treat madness. The author implies the reader is beyond help if they cannot understand this.</note>. Therefore, I will add nothing more, lest I make fools mad who currently believe they are wise.

This oil is of a singular and incredible sweetness, with which no other thing may be compared. It is pleasant to use, and all corrosiveness has been separated from it. No man can judge by thought, or comprehend by understanding, what incredible effects, potent powers, and profitable virtues are in this "Royal Oil." Therefore, to this sulfur of antimony, I have given no other name than my "Balsam of Life." It accomplishes very much, by the grace of God, in those for whom no help was hoped for except by the mercy of God, and for whom nothing remained but the certain expectation of trading life for death—as my brethren can witness has been often done. It refreshes a man as if he were newborn; it purifies the blood. When mixed and administered with the tincture of corals, it cleanses leprosy and expels every scab which takes root in man through the impurity of the blood. It drives away melancholy and sadness of heart; it strengthens the joints <note>original: "Junctures"</note>,

## Chariot of Antimony.

Take out the precipitate and grind it into a fine powder. Spread this upon a flat and clean stone and set it in a cold, moist place, leaving it there for six months. At length, the precipitate begins to dissolve itself into a red and pure liquor, and the dregs or earth are separated from it. The <term>Salt of Antimony</term> <note>original: "Salt of Antimony"; in early chemistry, this refers to the soluble crystalline essence extracted from the mineral</note>, I say, is the only part that dissolves into liquor. Filter this and put it into a cucurbit <note>a gourd-shaped chemical vessel used for distillation</note> so that it may be thickened by the extraction of the watery phlegm. Set it again in a moist place, and it will yield you beautiful crystals. Separate these from their remaining moisture, and they will be transparent with a reddish tint; however, when they are purified again, they become white. Then the true <term>Salt of Antimony</term> is made, just as I have often prepared it.

<note>The Greek proverb *isos* <note>original: "ἴσος", meaning "equal" or "like"</note>, or "like with like," is clearly demonstrated here in our chemical work. First, a white spirit comes off, and afterward a red spirit, which also turns back into white. Rectify this spirit gently and carefully in a dry or moist *bath* <note>original: "Balneo"; a heat-controlled bath such as a sand bath or water bath</note>, and so you will have another white oil distilled from the Salt of Antimony. This oil—but why do I call it oil? It is very effective in breaking the <term>stone of the bladder</term>; it promotes urination and is beneficial for the gout. This signifies nothing other than the character of those who oppose me. They are ashamed of the labor of planting trees and grafting fruitful sprouts onto them; therefore, they always remain among dry and withered wild trees. They can never reach any branch of green, sappy, and well-cultivated fruit trees.

Do not hasten, I say—you who are experienced in our Art—to pass your judgment. Do not be willing to condemn what you have not yet acquired through your own thoughts or gained through disciplined study. Many unwise men frequently say that fishes are frozen in the water, but in saying this, they reveal their own imprudence and lack of knowledge. For it will never be proven that a fish, even in the bitterest winter, will ever be frozen in the water as long as the ice is daily broken by the diligence of mortals. 

The real reason why fishes die is because, when the ice is not opened, their respiration is hindered, and they are suffocated as a result. For it may easily be proved that no animal can live when the use of air is denied to it. From this, it may well be concluded that those fishes found dead under the water in an extremely cold season do not die of cold (as men of little understanding think), but because they are deprived of air. 

By similar reasoning (to apply this example), I say that since <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: "Antimony"; here referring to the mineral stibnite, which Valentine believed held the key to universal medicine</note> is meant to produce such admirable fruits, it must be taken out of the mountains. But first, by the care of the miners, <term>spiracles</term> <note>breathing holes or ventilation shafts in a mine</note> or breathing places must be made for it. Afterward, it must be prepared with water, air, and fire as auxiliary mediums, lest its fruitfulness be suffocated in the earth. Through many and laborious preparations of craftsmanship, it must be revealed and brought to light for the expected <term>sanation</term> <note>healing or curing</note> of diseases, by...

I wish man were so inclined that he would study to obtain something through labor and diligence; then certainly the gods, the presiding spirits of prosperity and art, would give success. Then would cease the many poorly-founded, irrelevant ramblings of babblers, which stun the ears of students in the schools,

That by these metaphors, to every searcher I may briefly propose my admonition and advice, I say: My friend the hunter, dispose rightly your nets and instruments for hunting as is required; and you mariner, who night and day sail through the vast seas, and are often driven here and there by the winds, give heed to the point of your compass, and undoubtedly you will reap profit, and not bring home your ship, swiftly returning, without great gain of merchandise.

But why do I treat of many things, or spend time unprofitably (as prattling sophisters <note>original: "Sophiſters"; here meaning those who use clever but fallacious arguments to deceive</note> are accustomed to do) in beating out the empty chaff? I am deceived;

## Chariot of Antimony

<note>original: "Chariot of Antimony" refers to the *Currus Triumphalis Antimonii*, a famous alchemical text attributed to Basil Valentine.</note>

I have perceived that I do not spend my time unprofitably; for all the words in my writings are of use, and in them are found few empty letters which do not contain some utility together with profitable instruction. Thus, the time I spend in writing will be a recreation rather than a burden to me. Therefore now, after the manner of fencers, I will step back one pace and introduce into the chemical laboratory a new doctrine of external things. Namely, I will show that antimony is of such a nature that it may be prepared to yield immediate help in medicine regarding external wounds, which present themselves to us in many forms and are addressed by surgery <note>original: "Chirurgy"</note>. Therefore, I will begin and briefly explain my processes—specifically, how it is to be used in medicine and how it may profitably be prepared.

Whosoever you are among the junior students who desire to search out the hidden <note>original: "Occult"</note> things of nature and to bring her secrets to light, attend to what I say, so that you may be able to distinguish day from night and what is clear from the obscure.

Take one part of Hungarian antimony, half a part of common salt, and six parts of unburnt clay <note>original: "Argilla"</note>. Grind them all together and distill vehemently with a continual fire without intermission. At length, an oil will come forth. From this, remove its watery residue <note>original: "Phlegm"</note> by distillation, so that a red, dry powder remains in the bottom of the gourd-shaped glass vessel <note>original: "Cucurbit"</note>. Grind this powder finely and dissolve it into a liquid upon a marble stone. You will then have a red, shining balsam for wounds, which far excels many other balsams. Its use is principally for wounds which have been open for a long time, and in the cure of which the doctors—with their plasters, ointments <note>original: "Unguents"</note>, oils, and bandages <note>original: "Ligaments"</note>—could achieve nothing.

<summary>The author introduces a practical medicinal recipe using Hungarian antimony, salt, and clay to create a red balsam specifically designed for treating chronic, stubborn external wounds.</summary>
