### The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

Lest one or another should judge me to do this out of ambition, or think that I hunt after fame—which I never sought nor desired, nor at this time (as I can holily affirm) is it aimed at by me—I will now give you the preparation of another oil.

Take "mortified" Mercury <note>original: "Mercury mortified"; mercury that has been chemically altered or "killed" so it is no longer fluid</note> (which is sublimed to a state of clarity and splendor and sold by apothecaries) and Antimony, in equal parts. Grind them together and distill them using a retort <note>a glass vessel with a long downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note> that will retain the spirits three times, and afterward rectify <note>purify by repeated distillation</note> this oil with spirit of wine <note>ethanol or high-proof alcohol</note>. Then the operation is finished, and the oil becomes red as blood; though at first it is white and like ice or congealed butter. This oil effects wonders in many conditions <note>original: "Affects"</note> where Nature gave no hope of improvement, and it almost always shows its force, virtue, power, and efficacy in the perfect transformation <note>original: "Emendation"</note> of evil into good.

By addition, another oil may be prepared that is very profitable for external wounds.

Take one part Antimony, one part Sulfur, half a part Sal-Ammoniac <note>ammonium chloride</note> or purified salt of urine, and two parts quicklime <note>original: "Calx-Vive"</note>. Forcefully extract the oil; whatever has sublimed <note>turned into vapor and solidified again</note>, grind that with the dregs <note>original: "Caput-mortuum"; the useless residue left after distillation</note>, and pour the distilled oil back over it. Distill it three times; then the oil is prepared.

When old wounds can in no way be healed, then use this oil. For it is strong, potent, and penetrative: and it lays a good foundation (just as oil of vitriol <note>sulfuric acid</note> does) for future healing <note>original: "Sanation"</note>.

An admirable balsam of various ingredients (among which is Antimony), very useful for old wounds, is prepared as follows:

Take four ounces <note>original: "℥iiij"</note> of Sulfur; set it over a moderate fire to melt, and put into it half a pound of Mercury...

Digest this extraction with *spirit of wine* in a well-closed *B.M.* for one month. 

Then abstract the *spirit of wine* just as you abstracted the *vinegar*, and put the remaining powder of wine into a flat glass dish. Set that dish in a cellar into a pail full of water, so that it may swim upon the water like a boat. By doing this, the powder which is in the glass will, in a few days, resolve itself into a clear and transparent liquor.

This liquor is healthy for old, open wounds and is a healing *balsam* in similar accidents if put into them and covered around with a common *styptic plaster* <note>a medical dressing used to stop bleeding</note>. In long-lasting <note>original: diuturnal</note> open wounds, it leaves no man without help, even in those cases where all other remedies have been tried in vain. Of open *ulcers* which have their origin from within the body, I do not speak here; for they cannot be perfectly healed without internal remedies.

Although at this time, few people are found who turn their thoughts this way, or take any course to touch the very root of those diseases of which I now treat.

If men would well consider in their minds the calamities of life into which the Fall of our first parents <note>Referring to Adam and Eve</note> plunged us, and seriously weigh that original sin and the great troop of evils issuing from it—namely, sadness, anguish, diseases, and miseries—they would certainly spend their time better. They would employ more labor to seek out the health of their neighbors, which was so strictly commended to them by the Supreme Ruler of Heaven and commanded by Him as their proper duty. 

But how many (it is a grief to say!) avoid labor and consume their time unprofitably. They do not do what they ought to do, but only what they have done before and still have a desire to do. They are afraid to do even as much as would soil their fingertips, as if they envied the tradesman who perhaps earns a small profit by selling soap—soap which they themselves would not willingly buy to wash their own delicate hands. 

Are we not all miserable mortals living here as strangers on the Earth, possessing nothing that we can truly call our own? Are not all things we use here the goods of our *Lord*, lent to us while we live and no longer? Therefore, we ought to behave ourselves in using these gifts so that, supported by a good conscience, we may be able to stand on that Day <note>The Day of Judgment</note> in which an account must be given for them. We must ensure we are not cast into prison and utter darkness for our ingratitude, where there shall be weeping and gnashing of teeth. Yet from this, few were made either better or more grateful to **GOD** for so great a benefit; instead, they persisted in derision and contempt of that which **GOD** will justify on the Last Day.

But enough of these matters for now. Let us proceed in our instruction of **Antimony**, from which yet another medicine may be prepared. I myself have experienced this to be very healing and effective in every kind of **fevers** and in the **pestilence** <note>The plague or other highly infectious epidemic diseases.</note>.

## The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

Grind <term>antimony</term> <note>original: "Antimony"; a metallic mineral used in alchemy and medicine</note> into a fine powder, put it into a glass retort, and distill it with a strong fire without adding anything else. Repeat this three or four times, always using a large receiving vessel. Eventually, it will be turned into a red powder. Extract the essence of this powder with vinegar, and circulate <note>original: "circulate"; a process of continuous distillation and condensation in a closed vessel</note> the extraction over a gentle fire for ten full days. Remove the vinegar by distilling it off. Through a unique * artifice in distilling, the remaining substance will be changed into an <term>oil</term>. Let this <term>oil</term> be further circulated until all moisture is drawn away from it and it is again reduced to a powder, as it was before. When the vinegar is removed and separated by distillation, then collect the <term>oil</term> in a new receiver.

<margin>* This extraction may be made volatile with spirit of wine <note>original: "Spirit of Wine"; high-proof alcohol or ethanol</note> in the same manner as I taught you in the previous operations.</margin>

Four grains of this <term>oil</term> taken with the water of <term>blessed thistle</term> <note>original: "Carduus Benedictus"; a plant used historically to treat fevers and the plague</note> will heal <term>quotidian, tertian, and quartan fevers</term> <note>fevers that recur every day, every third day, or every fourth day</note>, provided the sick person is well-covered and made to sweat. The same dose is very effective for expelling the <term>plague</term> <note>original: "Pest"</note>, whether given with spirit of wine or with distilled vinegar, depending on whether the <term>paroxysm</term> <note>a sudden attack or increase of symptoms</note> of the plague first attacks with heat or with cold.

This is witnessed by three brothers in our monastery, who recovered from the plague by this <term>secret remedy</term> <note>original: "Arcanum"</note> when they expected nothing but death and had already made their wills. This success so reconciled their minds to my art that they helped me with greater zeal than before, both through their prayers and their labor. They spent the leisure time they had—when not occupied by their religious duties—in serving me daily. In a short time, they attained such great experience that by their own industry, and the industry of their brothers, they gained more true knowledge in searching out the <term>secret mysteries</term> <note>original: "Arcanums"</note> of nature...

![An engraving depicting a chemical apparatus for sublimation or distillation. A large, tear-drop shaped glass vessel stands upright. Its narrow neck at the top is tied with a decorative ribbon. The wider, open bottom of the vessel rests on a small, square-shaped container, which in turn sits on a footed cup or crucible. This cup is placed on a round, flat plate or saucer. Smoke or vapor rises from the square container into the glass vessel, filling its interior with cloudy patterns. Additional smoke escapes from the narrow neck at the top.]

### The Method of Sublimation

The figure above demonstrates the proper arrangement of the vessels for the process of **sublimation** <note>original: "sublimation"; the process of heating a solid substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again on a cool surface</note>. 

As you may observe, the large glass vessel—often called a *cucurbit* or a *bolt-head*—is positioned directly over the heat source. The vapors, or "spirits," are drawn upward by the warmth of the fire below. It is essential that the neck of the glass be narrow to concentrate the rising steam, yet it must remain slightly open at the top, as indicated by the escaping smoke, to prevent the pressure from shattering the glass.

<margin>The ribbon signifies the sealing of the joints.</margin>

The decorative ribbon tied around the neck serves as a reminder to the practitioner to ensure all connections are secure, though one must always allow for the "breath of the stone" <note>a metaphorical term for the expanding gases in alchemical experiments</note> to find its way. 

#### Observations on the Process

1.  **The Base:** The square container holds the raw material, which is placed within a crucible or footed cup to ensure even distribution of heat.
2.  **The Vapor:** Note how the clouds fill the belly of the glass. This represents the purification of the matter as it leaves its earthly dross <note>impurities or waste matter</note> behind.
3.  **The Collection:** Once the heat is removed, the purified substance will adhere to the internal sides of the upper glass, appearing like a fine frost or "snow."

<note>original: "Spirits are not bound by earthly weight."</note>

Through this art, the heavy becomes light, and the solid becomes spiritual. One must watch the fire with great care, for too much heat will burn the flowers <note>original: "flores"; the fine powder produced by sublimation</note>, while too little will fail to raise them at all.

<summary>This page illustrates and explains the apparatus used for chemical sublimation, detailing how heat transforms solid matter into purified vapors that collect in a glass vessel.</summary>
<keywords>sublimation, alchemy, laboratory apparatus, distillation, cucurbit, purification, chemical history</keywords>

## Chariot of Antimony.

We use three or four drops of this, taken internally with spirit of wine <note>original: "Spirit of Wine"; ethanol or high-proof alcohol</note>, against the phthisis <note>original: "Phthisick"; a wasting disease, usually pulmonary tuberculosis</note> of the lungs. However, used externally—if the area is first anointed and a styptic plaster <note>original: "Stiptick Playster"; a medical dressing designed to stop bleeding</note> is applied—it works against all stinking wounds that tend toward putrefaction. You will find it to be the most certain remedy for all wounds.

Furthermore, another oil of antimony, which is very profitable against all corroding wounds, is made in this way:

Take one ounce <note>symbol: ℥. j.</note> of antimony, half an ounce <note>symbol: ℥. ß.</note> of dried common salt, and five ounces <note>symbol: ℥. v.</note> of broken tiles. Grind them all together and put them into a retort <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note>, from which you shall distill a yellow oil. When all the spirits have come forth, put the matter into another glass vessel and extract the phlegm <note>the watery, non-active distillate</note> from it. A powder will remain; spread this upon a stone in a humid place, and you will obtain a liquid balsam. This is a singular remedy for all "verminant" wounds <note>wounds infested with maggots or parasites</note> and for cancer, especially those occurring on a man's face or a woman's breasts. Much more might be written of this balsam, did I not fear that every unskillful man and the rabble of sophisters <note>original: "Sophisters"; here meaning fraudulent or overly-theoretical scholars who lack practical experience</note> would attack me and say I speak too grandly, committing more to writing than experience has taught me, and that I boast only of speculations and mere imaginations.

Moreover, another oil is made in this manner:

Sublime <note>to heat a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again</note> one part of antimony with a fourth part of sal ammoniac <note>ammonium chloride</note> using a subtle fire. The salt carries up the sulfur of antimony, which is as red as blood. Grind this sublimate to a fine powder. If you took one ounce of antimony at first, grind it again with five ounces of sal ammoniac and sublime as before. Dissolve the sublimate in a moist place. Or otherwise, take the sublimate and wash away the added salt <note>original: "edulcorate it"; to purify or sweeten by rinsing</note>, gently dry it, and you will have sulfur, which...

That first essence...

## Chariot of Antimony.
<note>original: "Charlot" is likely a typographical error for "Chariot," referring to the "Triumphant Chariot of Antimony" by Basil Valentine.</note>

<margin>* Here it is to be noted: In the third fusion of the Regulus <note>The metallic mass that sinks to the bottom during smelting</note>, the fire must be vehemently heightened, so that if any impurity remains with the Regulus, it may be taken away by that intense heat. By this means, you will have a Regulus comparable to silver in beauty and whiteness, but far superior in virtue and price.</margin>

I sincerely declare that it is not as some say, for those kinds of seekers wander from the King’s highway. They kill themselves by clambering up rocks and cliffs where wild goats live and birds of prey build their nests. It is not granted to this "Star" to contain such great potency within itself, or to form such a precious stone on its own. Yet I affirm that within it lies hidden <note>original: "abfconded"</note> a famous medicine which may be made from it. 

The Star is made in this way: Take three parts of Hungarian Antimony and one part of steel. Melt these together with four parts of burnt Tartar <note>Potassium bitartrate, used as a flux</note>. When melted, pour the whole mixture into a cone-shaped mold. When it is cold, take out the Regulus and separate it from all impurity and the slag <note>original: "Scoria"</note>. Grind this Regulus to powder and weigh it; then add to it three times as much burnt Tartar and pour it out as before. Repeat this labor a third time; then the Regulus purges itself and becomes pure and clear. 

Note: when you have rightly completed the fusion and have used proper manual skill (which is of principal concern in this work), you will obtain a fair Star, bright and shining like refined silver <note>original: "Cupellate Silver," referring to silver purified in a cupel</note>. It will be no less artistically formed than if a painter had diligently divided it with his compasses.

This Star, when combined with Sal Ammoniac <note>Ammonium chloride</note>, is reduced to a red sublimate <note>A solid deposit produced by heating a substance into a vapor</note>, for the essence of iron <note>original: "Tincture of Mars"</note> ascends. Such a sublimate may be dissolved in a moist place into a liquid, which reveals wonderful virtues in surgery <note>original: "Chirurgy"</note>.

<page-num>136</page-num>
# Basil Valentine's Triumphant Chariot of Antimony

<margin>* This sublimate <note>a solid substance produced by heating a material until it vaporizes and then cools back into a solid</note>, before it is set in a cellar to be dissolved there, should be purged of the Sal Ammoniac <note>ammonium chloride, used as a flux in alchemy</note> with distilled water. I give only a few warnings, but through ignorance of these details, great errors are committed. The work, along with all its costs and expenses, perishes—or at least fails to yield enough to pay for the materials.</margin>

This Regulus <note>the metallic heart or "little king" of the mineral</note>, or Signate Star, when melted often with the Stony Serpent, is brought to such a state that it eventually consumes itself within it and wholly unites itself with the Serpent. * When this is done, the practitioner <note>original: "sessator," a sitter or one who stays with the work</note> of Art has a substance that is altogether hot and fiery, in which much of the Art is hidden. This prepared matter dissolves itself into an oil; this very oil ought to be brought over the helm <note>the top part of a still</note> by distillation and then rectified <note>purified by repeated distillation</note> so that it may be pure and clear.

<margin>* The nature of a snake or serpent is such that if you loosen your grip, it rises up, but if you squeeze it too hard, it bursts; I fear the same happens here. Therefore, the author calls that which he mixes with this Regulus a "Serpent." But it is the "Serpent of a Stone" or a "Stony Serpent" because the salt, like a snake, willingly licks a stone.</margin>

This oil may conveniently and securely be taken internally, but with great prudence and caution. It should be taken no more than twice or thrice a week, and no more at one time than three drops in two drams <note>symbol: ʒij; approximately 7.5 milliliters</note> of wine or other water distilled from herbs, according to the urgency of the disease. For this reason, it is the physician's duty to know the causes of diseases, along with the temperament of the sick person, so that he may use his remedies more securely.

This is a famous sharpness <note>original: "acrimony"</note> containing within itself many secrets <note>original: "arcanums"</note>; but there is no need to reveal everything all at once to unskilled men. Some arts are to be suppressed so that certain secrets and mysteries may remain the private property of the philosopher <note>in this context, an alchemist or seeker of natural wisdom</note>.

# Chariot of Antimony

<note>The author refers to himself as a philosopher</note> who, in searching out these secrets, has daily sustained grievous toil.

***

Those who understand how great a utility it is to extract the essences from metals are not ignorant of the virtues of this sharp oil. For this is the only solvent <note>original: "Menstruum"</note> for this purpose. How many people are there who have spent their whole lives in chemical operations and could never arrive at the knowledge of a true solvent? To you, it is revealed here: if health is your aim, you may safely use it in the body; if you attempt something more sublime and have already conceived a good hope that you shall compound it, this is the principal help of all for ascending to the thrones of the chemical kingdom.

But let him who resolves to tread in my footsteps not be weary of searching. What I have done, let him do; and what I have so often desired, and what with such earnest wishes I have sought, let him seek. These principles which I have prescribed for you are sufficient to search out the ultimate goal. Many have failed—indeed, many have been cut off by death—before they could, in their learning, attain even to the principles; that is, they were deprived of life before they could acquire the mastery of the art <note>original: "Magistery of Art," referring to the highest level of alchemical skill or the production of the Philosopher's Stone</note>. Therefore, I first set forth a book of rudiments <note>basic principles</note> so that the studious follower of art (who in his first experiences had need of so much time) might the sooner attain his desired end and wished-for goal, and next unto God, give me thanks.

Moreover, a wonderful effect lies hidden in this oil. For if this oil is circulated with crystals for some time—namely, for three days and nights (the crystals being first calcined <note>reduced to powder by heat</note>)—it extracts a salt from them. When this is done, the oil may again be distilled off by a retort <note>a glass vessel with a long downward-pointing neck used for distilling</note>. Thus you will have a medicine which admirably breaks the stone in the bladder and expels it; and also effects...

--- Page 176 ---
<meta>This page continues Basil Valentine’s discourse on the chemical properties of Antimony and its relationship to other metals, specifically Lead.</meta>

<page-num>138</page-num>
<header>Basil Valentine's Triumphant Chariot of Antimony</header>

It effects many other things by a certain famous virtue within it.

But so that we may also say something of the *Lead* of Philosophers <note>The "Lead of the Philosophers" refers to a specialized chemical substance used in alchemy, distinct from common lead</note>, let the curious searchers of Nature know that between *Antimony* and common *Lead*, there is a certain near affinity, and they hold a strict friendship with each other. Just as a tree casts out from its side its superfluous *rosin* <note>original: "Rosin"; resin</note>, which is the *sulphur* of that tree; or as the cherry tree and other trees give forth such *gums*; there are other kinds of trees also which, by reason of their abundance of *mercury*, produce and cast forth from themselves a certain *excrescence* <note>An abnormal growth or byproduct</note>. This byproduct is in no way like their *fruit* in either form or virtue, but has entirely different properties, as is apparent in *oaks* and *apple trees*, which produce similar bastard *fruits* or *monsters* <note>Likely referring to galls or mistletoe</note>. In the same way, the *Earth* also has similar abortive *fruits*, which are severed and cast out during the separation from the pure *metals*.

Now, although there is such a strict affinity between *Antimony* and *Saturn* <note>Saturn is the alchemical name for Lead</note>, yet by reason of the excessive *sulphur* which *Antimony* has within itself, it is cast out from it. This is because its viscous *body* (at its *birth*) could not come to perfection; therefore, it was necessarily constrained to remain among the *minerals*. Its abundance of hot *sulphur* was the cause that hindered its *mercury*, so that through a lack of *cold*, it could neither reach coagulation nor become a malleable *body* <note>A metal that can be hammered or shaped</note>. 

Moreover, I say that the *lead* in *Antimony* is nothing other than its *regulus* <note>The metallic heart or purest part of the mineral that settles at the bottom during smelting</note>, which has not yet obtained *malleability*. And, as I said above, when the *regulus* and *steel* are united by *liquefaction* <note>Melting</note> and reduced to a *star* <note>Referring to the "Star Regulus of Antimony," a crystalline pattern that forms on the surface of the metal</note>, there are many who would try to make the ancient Stone of the Philosophers from it; which I previously denied to be possible. Yet

<summary>Basil Valentine explains the alchemical relationship between Antimony and Lead (Saturn), comparing mineral byproducts to the resins and growths of trees. He argues that Antimony is an "abortive" metal that failed to become malleable due to an excess of sulphur.</summary>

### The Triumphal Chariot of <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: "Antimony" refers here to the mineral stibnite and its various chemical derivatives.</note>.

What medicines may be prepared from it, you have already briefly heard; therefore, regarding them, I shall not add a word more.

<margin>* I do not deal *enviously*, as many <term>chemists</term> do, but *affectionately* with <term>surgeons</term> <note>original: "Chirurgeons"</note>: wishing that they would in their minds—as according to their profession they may and ought—endeavor to prepare such helps for their miserably afflicted <term>patients</term>, and such <term>shortcuts to health</term> <note>original: "Compendiums of Sanity"</note>, as may be prepared from this <term>regulus</term> <note>the metallic heart or purest part of a mineral produced by smelting</note>. Would you have me discover the <term>mystery</term> to you? Listen with pleasure, and use it. This <term>regulus</term>, rendered malleable by the <term>salt of lead</term> <note>original: "Salt of Saturn"; Saturn is the alchemical symbol for lead</note>, must be mixed with equal parts of <term>mercury</term> condensed by <term>lead</term>, and melted in a vehement fire until well mixed. The matter comes forth looking like <term>silver</term> on the outside, but in its internal virtues, it is more noble and more precious than any <term>silver</term>. But you, <term>surgeon</term>, being a student of your own art and desiring glory through that art, beat that metal into thin plates and apply it externally to <term>wounds</term> and <term>malignant fistulas</term> <note>deep, infected sores or abnormal passages in the body</note>. By doing so, you will be amazed when you see <term>Nature</term>, helped by this art, perform more in a very short time than you could have hoped for in a longer time using so many <term>ointments</term> <note>original: "unguents"</note> and <term>plasters</term>. The <term>peasants</term> <note>original: "Rusticks"</note> (to use the words of <term>Basilius</term> <note>referring to Basilius Valentinus, the purported author</note>) will no longer mock and insult you, saying they can achieve more with a piece of raw and stinking <term>lard</term> than you are able to do with the laborious processes of your entire <term>surgical art</term>.</margin>

But the reason why the <term>regulus</term> is called and accounted <term>lead</term> is this: when that <term>regulus</term> is taken—which <term>antimony</term> gives off during the making of <term>glass</term>—and put into a well-closed <term>crucible</term> that can resist the fire, along with the <term>salt of lead</term> (having first been bonded with the salt for three hours), and these are permitted to melt together in a <term>blast furnace</term> <note>original: "Wind Furnace"</note>, the <term>regulus</term>, when taken out, is found to be rendered soft and heavier than it was before. For it receives its weight from the <term>spirit of the salt</term>, which also gave it softness, so that its body is now compact and heavy.

Therefore I say, there is not much difference between the <term>signed star</term> <note>original: "Signate Star"; a crystalline pattern that forms on the surface of purified antimony regulus</note> and the <term>lead of antimony</term>; which, despite this, are everywhere distinguished as two different things. For either of them is made from the <term>glass of antimony</term> and prepared in...

->APPENDIX.<-

For a conclusion, you should further know that <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: "Antimony"; a semi-metallic element used in both medicine and metallurgy</note> may be applied to many other uses than those expressed above; such as for writing or printing, for which printers use it. Also, under a certain <term>constellation</term> and <term>concourse of the planets</term> <note>The alignment of stars and planets believed to influence the success of alchemical or metallurgical work</note>, a <term>mixture of metals</term> is made with antimony, from which artists form <term>signatures</term> and <term>characters</term> <note>Talismans or symbolic engravings believed to hold spiritual or physical power</note> endowed with singular virtues. From the same mixture, <term>speculums</term> <note>Mirrors, often used in this context for divination or optical experiments</note> are also made, possessing many wonderful appearances and properties. Likewise, <term>bells</term> and other <term>instruments</term> may be made from it, having an admirable sound. Similarly, <term>images of men</term> and many other things can be created.

## The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

The virtues and powers of Antimony, which the author <note>Basil Valentine</note> touches upon so lightly and passes over in this appendix, are so many and so varied that the hundredth part of them is not yet known to men. This ignorance undoubtedly results in the reproach and shame of our species. Because we humans—who alone among so many other animals are endowed with reason and the faculty of speech—are driven with such great impulsiveness by that wicked and abominable desire for profit, scarcely any man has the leisure to search out the wonders which the Author of Nature <note>God</note> has instilled in His creatures. I am unwilling to repeat this reprimand so often spoken of by Valentine; I only call it to mind. 

This mineral, in which lies hidden such an admirable spirit that it cannot be exhausted by use, also has virtues which no man can sufficiently know through study. What I have tested outside the fields of chemistry <note>original: "Chymistry"</note> and medicine are few; yet experience has taught me enough that I judge Antimony will show itself no less admirable in other things than it does in chemistry and medicine.

However, what I think of characters and signatures <note>term: "Signatures" refers to the Doctrine of Signatures, the belief that herbs and minerals have physical features indicating their medicinal or spiritual use</note>, which the author says may be made under a gathering of certain constellations, I shall not reveal here. It is enough for me to say that among all metals and minerals, there is no known substance which contains so much of a celestial spirit <note>original: "Cœlestial Spirit"</note> and has such great sympathy with the stars as Antimony. Weigh this carefully with all that I have said before regarding Antimony—do not do so negligently—and hasten toward the stone which is called the Stone of Fire.

But since these things do not concern medicine, nor pertain to my order, rule, and calling, I remain well satisfied in my vocation and commend these matters to the handling of others who know them better.

<summary>The commentator reflects on the inexhaustible spiritual and astrological virtues of Antimony, lamenting that human greed prevents deeper study of its celestial connections and the "Stone of Fire."</summary>

### What the STONE of FIRE is.

<margin>* This permission is to be granted to all who treat of sacred things: namely, to declare those things which they are willing to reveal—not to the unlearned, ignorant scoffers, but only to men who are worthy, and to those who sincerely desire and seek the truth.</margin>

When, at a certain time, an abundance of thoughts (which my internal and fervent prayer to God suggested) had set me loose and wholly free from all earthly <note>original: "terrene"</note> businesses, I purposed within myself to attend to spiritual inspirations. We have need of these for a more accurate investigation <note>original: "scrutiny"</note> of Nature. Therefore, I resolved to make myself wings, so that I might ascend on high and inspect the stars themselves, just as Icarus and his father Daedalus did in times past, if credit may be given to the ancient writings of the poets.

### The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

The work of those who imitate nature was (as I may say) more true than Truth itself. Therefore, as is fitting, I give praise and thanks to my *Lord* and *Heavenly Father* for his incomprehensible works.

In very deed (to explain the matter in few words), I found all things which are generated in the bowels of mountains to be infused from the *superior stars*. They take their beginning from those stars in the form of a *watery* <note>original: "aqueous"</note> cloud, smoke, or vapor. For a very long time, this vapor is fed and nourished by the stars, until it is finally brought out <note>original: "educted"</note> into a tangible form by the elements. Moreover, this vapor is dried so that the wateriness may lose its dominance, and then the fire—with the help of the air—retains the ruling power. From water, fire is produced; and from fire and air, earth is produced. Notwithstanding, these elements are found in all things consisting of a body before they are separated. Therefore, this—namely, water—is the first matter of all things, which is formed into earth by the dryness of fire and air.

<gloss>This is an old song; this is the sum of the Art. From this *imitation of nature* the lesser *Stone of Fire* is found; from this it is made whenever it is prepared. From this same source, the great *Philosophical Stone* also derives its origin. This is the water of *Anaxagoras* <note>Anaxagoras was a Pre-Socratic Greek philosopher who proposed that all things existed in a primordial mixture of "seeds."</note>, and to this day things are made from it. This is clearly evident in the nutrition of man, the growth of a tree, and in the generation of metals. For that *which constitutes flesh, wood, and metals* is not taken from food, rain, or earth, but *is infused into them from elsewhere*. That very thing is the nourishment which feeds all things; but for it to be so variously specified, it must be separated from that body in which it dwells and be joined to another, *which is performed by the chemical art*.</gloss>

But now, since my intention is to describe the *Stone of Fire*—how it is made of *Antimony*, together with the process of its preparation—which not only heats men but also metals specifically, it will be necessary before all things to speak some—

<summary>The author explains that all matter originates as a celestial vapor infused by the stars into the earth, eventually solidifying into elements through a natural process that the alchemist must imitate. He introduces his specific focus on creating the "Stone of Fire" from Antimony.</summary>
<keywords>Antimony, Stone of Fire, First Matter, Alchemy, Anaxagoras, Four Elements, Celestial Influence, Generation of Metals</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Basil Valentine’s discourse on the "Stone of Fire," specifically focusing on its origins and its relationship to Antimony.</meta>

# Basil Valentine's Triumphant Chariot of Antimony

...what of these following heads: What properly the Stone of Fire is; what is its mineral source <note>original: "Minera"</note>; whether a stone can be made without matter or not; what is the extreme difference between stones, and how many species of them are found; and lastly, of their use.

*In this my purpose, I pray, O Spirit of Heaven, illuminate me, that I may give a true and sincere instruction—namely, according as is fit for me, and as the matter itself permits. Indeed, I have hopes of eternal absolution from this my supreme confessor, who from eternity possesses the throne of mercy, and will give testimony of all things when the final sentence <note>original: "Decretory Sentence"</note> shall be pronounced upon all men in the Last Judgment, without any appeal.*

Therefore, first know and consider that the true tincture of <term>Antimony</term> <note>A metallic element often used in alchemy to represent the process of purification</note>, which is the medicine of men and metals, is not made of crude and melted antimony as it is sold by merchants and apothecaries. Instead, it is extracted from the mineral ore as it is taken out of the mountains, and before it is formed into glass. But how that extraction should be made is the principal work in which the whole art consists. Health and riches attend him who rightly attains to that. 

But, my reader, you must diligently mind this: namely, that the tincture of antimony—prepared, fixed, and solid, or the "Stone of Fire" (as I name it)—is a certain pure, penetrative, spiritual, and fiery essence. It is reduced into a coagulated matter, like the salamander <note>In alchemy, the salamander was a mythical creature believed to live in and endure fire without being harmed</note>, which is not consumed in fire, but rather purified and preserved.

Yet the Stone of Fire does not perform universal transformation <note>original: "tingeth not universally"</note> like the *Stone of the Philosophers*, which is made of the essence of gold itself. To this [Stone of Fire] no such power is given that it should perform such things; rather, it transforms <note>original: "tingeth"</note> particularly: namely, silver into gold, and tin also...

<summary>Basil Valentine defines the "Stone of Fire" as a fiery essence extracted from raw antimony ore, distinguishing it from the universal Philosophers' Stone while noting its power to transform specific metals like silver and tin into gold.</summary>
<keywords>Basil Valentine, Stone of Fire, Antimony, Tincture, Alchemy, Mineral Ore, Salamander, Transmutation, Gold, Silver</keywords>

--- Page 185 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Stone of Fire" derived from Antimony and its comparative power in alchemical transmutation.</meta>

### The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

It also affects Lead; but *Mars* <note>Iron</note> and *Venus* <note>Copper</note> it does not touch, nor do they yield more than what can be produced from them by separation.

<margin>* Just as much as Heaven is elevated above the Earth, so much does the true Stone of the Philosophers differ from this Stone of Fire. I myself candidly confess that although I have found this, I am still very far distant from the other. And this, whatever it is, I acknowledge was received from the wisdom of Basilius <note>Basil Valentine, the purported author</note>. Take care that you are not deluded by your own fantasy, and that others do not deceive you.</margin>

Moreover, one part of it can tinge <note>term: Tinge; to transform or change the color and nature of a base metal into gold or silver</note> no more than five parts of metal, so as to endure the trial of *Saturn* <note>Lead</note> and *Antimony*; whereas, on the contrary, the Great Stone of the Philosophers can transmute to infinity. Also, in augmentation <note>The process of increasing the quantity or potency of the alchemical agent</note> it cannot be exalted so far; yet the gold it produces is pure and solid.

The *Minera* <note>original: Minera; the raw ore or mineral source</note>, out of which this Stone or Tincture is made, is no other than (as I mentioned above) the very Earth of *Antimony*; from which, I say, it is made. But how, or with what virtue, force, and power it is endowed, you shall hear shortly.

Let the reader consider that there are many kinds of stones found which tinge specifically; however, I signify all fixed powders which tinge by the name of "Stones." Yet one tinges more highly than another: especially the Stone of the Philosophers, which holds the principal place; the next is the Tincture of *Sol* <note>Gold</note>; and of *Luna* <note>Silver</note>, etc., for the White Work. After these follow the Tincture of *Vitriol* or *Venus* <note>Copper</note>; likewise the Tincture of *Mars* <note>Iron</note>; either of which has within itself the Tincture of *Sol* when reduced to fixation <note>term: Fixation; the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat</note>. Next to these follow the Tinctures of *Jupiter* <note>Tin</note> and *Saturn* <note>Lead</note> for the coagulation of *Mercury*; and lastly, the Tincture of *Mercury* itself. This is the difference and multiplicity of Stones and Tinctures, all of which notwithstanding...

<summary>The author distinguishes the "Stone of Fire" derived from antimony from the "Great Stone of the Philosophers," noting that while the former produces pure gold, its power of transmutation is limited. He categorizes various alchemical tinctures associated with specific metals and their hierarchical potency.</summary>

<keywords>Antimony, Stone of Fire, Philosophers' Stone, Transmutation, Tincture, Basilius Valentinus, Alchemy, Metallurgy, Sol, Luna, Mars, Venus</keywords>

--- Page 186 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the origins of the Philosopher's Stone and the specific roles of minerals and salts in the alchemical process.</meta>

<page-num>148</page-num>
<header>Basil Valentine's Triumphant Chariot</header>

All metals are generated from seed and from one original *Matrix* <note>original: "Matrix"; here meaning a womb or formative substance</note>, from which the true *Universal Stone* proceeds; however, out of these, no other *Metallic Tincture* <note>A "tincture" in alchemy is a substance capable of transforming or "coloring" base metals into gold or silver</note> is to be found. As for all other things, by whatever name they are called—all *stones* (whether precious or common)—I will not touch upon them now, nor do I have any intention to write or speak anything of them at this time. This is because they contain in themselves no other *virtues* <note>original: "Virtues"; refers to inherent medicinal or transformative powers</note> than those which appertain to *medicine*. Nor shall I mention here *animal* or *vegetable stones*, because they are only conducive to *medicine*; for *metallic works*, they are unprofitable and void of all *virtue*. Yet all the *virtues* of all things—*mineral, animal, and vegetable*—when collected into one, are found in the *Stone of the Philosophers*.

*Salts* are endowed with no transforming *virtue* on their own, but are only *keys* for the preparation of *stones*; otherwise, by themselves, they effect nothing.

<margin>Salts, as is rightly said here, are *keys*; they open the *chest* wherein the *treasure* lies. But you must be sure to take the true *key*; otherwise, you may *spoil* the *lock* and not open the *chest*. It is not safe in this case to take one thing for another <note>original: "Quid for Quo"; a variation of "quid pro quo," meaning a substitution</note>, as *apothecaries* are accustomed to doing. You must have a *philosophical key*, and proper *salts* fit for opening must be used. Nor should you ignore that *distinction* which is suggested between *opening salts*—which the author here calls "keys"—and *fixing salts*, which enter the treasure itself, as is sufficiently clear by the *text*.</margin>

Yet, regarding *metallic salts* (I now speak to the purpose, if you rightly understand what *distinction* I make between *mineral salts*), they are not to be lightly esteemed, nor are they to be rejected in *tinctures*. We can in no way do without them in their *composition*, for in them lies that most precious *treasure* from which every *fixation* <note>The process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire</note> derives its *origin*.

Here some may ask, and indeed very properly: whether such a *stone* can be made without matter?

<summary>Basil Valentine explains that while medicinal properties exist in many substances, only specific metallic seeds can produce the Philosopher's Stone, emphasizing that salts act as essential "keys" to unlock these metallic treasures.</summary>
<keywords>Basil Valentine, Matrix, Universal Stone, Metallic Tincture, Alchemy, Salts, Fixing Salts, Opening Salts, Fixation, Mineral vs Vegetable Stones</keywords>

--- Page 187 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the chemical and spiritual preparation of the Philosopher's Stone, specifically focusing on the transition from physical matter to a spiritual essence and back to a solid form.</meta>

### Chariot of Antimony.

I answer, No. For every thing must have its own matter, but not without *distinction*. *Animals* require their *matter*, *vegetables* theirs, and *minerals* theirs. Only consider and before all things observe this: namely, that no body can be useful for any Stone without *fermentation*. I find that at the end of the work (I mean regarding the preparation of the Great Stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the ultimate goal of alchemy</note>) this cannot be omitted if I wish to convert *metals* with profit. For although in the beginning a physical form and physical entity—visible and tangible—is taken, yet from that formal body must be extracted a certain spiritual and celestial entity (shall I call it?) or appearance; for I find no other more fit name to give it. This entity was previously infused into that body by the *stars* and concocted and made perfect by the *elements*. Yet this spiritual entity must again, by a lesser fire and by the *regimen* <note>original: "regimen"; the systematic method or rule of treatment</note> and direction of the *microcosm* <note>The "little world," referring here to the alchemist or the human being as a reflection of the universe</note>, be reduced to a tangible, fixed, solid, and indestructible matter.

<margin>Are you well in your wits, *Basilius*, who thus prostituted the *secret* <note>original: "Arcanum"</note> of the *Stone*, which has until this day been so diligently hidden by all *philosophers*? Surely, if you had not labored with some unknown *intemperance* of mind, you would have closed your lips and not have so clearly opened what it is to separate the pure from the impure; how to render the fixed volatile, and again to fix that; how the inferior becomes superior, and that again plunged into the deep *abyss* from which it had ascended. To discover so many *mysteries* in so few words as you do here—assuredly, if it is not madness, it is a certain very great benevolence to *posterity*. This is what moved him. *Valentine* seems to have burned with this affection and could not overcome it.</margin>

But what am I doing, or what am I speaking? I act as if I were deprived of my reason in uttering words so openly. For if I had either reason or judgment, I should not discover such great things with my tongue, or command my hand to proceed in writing them.

<summary>Basilius Valentine describes the alchemical process of extracting a spiritual essence from physical matter and re-fixing it into a solid Stone, while a marginal note marvels at his willingness to reveal such guarded secrets.</summary>
<keywords>Basilius Valentine, Philosopher's Stone, fermentation, spiritual entity, microcosm, alchemy, arcanum, transmutation</keywords>

<page-num>150</page-num>
# Basil Valentine's Triumphant Chariot

That inclination toward doing good for many is restrained by the obligation (imposed on all philosophers) of concealing that secret mystery of nature. This mystery is communicated only to the worthy and chosen "Sons of Art" <note>A traditional term for alchemists who have mastered the "Great Work."</note> by its author—that holy Nature, which acts as a creative force <note>original: "naturating"</note> through intellectual revelation. The "Secret of Secrets" has been revealed by Basilius <note>Basil Valentine</note>; you, the reader, must pay attention. If you find the pearl, do not be like Aesop's cock <note>A reference to Aesop's fable where a rooster finds a precious jewel but prefers a grain of corn, signifying those who value trivialities over wisdom.</note>.

All tinctures <note>In alchemy, a "tincture" is a potent substance capable of transmuting or "coloring" base metals into gold or silver.</note> of metals must be separated in such a way that they are moved by a certain primary love and affection toward metals. They must have a longing and desire to unite themselves with them and to reduce them to a better state. Do you want an emblem or an example? Behold, here is the example of man and woman. If these two are inflamed with mutual love, neither delay nor rest is permitted until they are united and their desire is satisfied. After this union, they rest and are multiplied, according to the good pleasure of God and the promise of His blessing.

Man lives subject to many and perilous diseases, some of which weaken and consume the powers of nature so much that the man can be perfectly restored to health and his former strength by no remedies. But love is a disease with which no other disease may be compared. It is not healed except by the production of its own likeness, which either sex desires; and that desire is not satisfied except by fulfilling the will of that kindled affection. How many testimonies of this violence found in love are discovered daily? For it not only inflames the younger sort, but it so disturbs <note>original: "exagitates"</note> some persons far gone in years that, through its burning heat, they are almost driven mad. Natural diseases are for the most part governed by the temperament <note>original: "complexion"</note> of a man, and therefore invade some more fiercely and others more gently; but love, without distinction of poor or rich, young or old, seizes all. Having seized them, it so blinds them that...

<summary>Basil Valentine explains that alchemical "tinctures" must have a natural affinity for metals, using the intense, blinding power of human love and biological reproduction as a metaphor for chemical union and transmutation.</summary>
<keywords>Basil Valentine, alchemy, Sons of Art, Secret of Secrets, Aesop's Cock, tinctures, transmutation, metaphor of love, temperament, medical philosophy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's philosophical and medical discourse on the nature of human passions, specifically focusing on the destructive power of "inordinate love" as a metaphorical disease.</meta>

<page-num>151</page-num>
<header>Chariot of Antimony.</header>

## Chariot of Antimony.

Forgetting all rules of reason, they neither see nor fear any snare. While specific body parts may be infected with the unique symptoms of other diseases—leaving all other parts healthy and free from that pain <note>original: "dolour"</note>—love is different. When love infects a person, it invades the entire being; it penetrates the body and its whole substance, form, and essence, leaving nothing unharmed. 

For once it takes root in the heart, it kindles a fire there. The burning heat of this fire is spread through the veins, arteries, and all the members of the body. In a word, I say that wherever love has once fixed its root, the man is so deprived of all sense, reason, and understanding that he forgets all things and can mind nothing seriously. He is unmindful of God and His Law; he regards His promises and threats very little; he even despises the torments of Hell and the rewards of eternal life. 

I speak here of inordinate and unlawful love. If a man is once addicted to this, he clings to it so stubbornly <note>original: "pertinaciously"</note> that nothing can reclaim him and nothing can restrain him. He forgets his duty, his calling <note>term: "calling" refers to one's divine or social vocation and profession</note>, and his social standing. He derides all warnings and despises the counsel of parents, superiors, and others who wish him well. Briefly, I say he is so blinded by love that he cannot see his own misery; he is so deaf that he cannot hear those who, through their faithful advice, endeavor to turn him away from the damage and evil that will befall him. 

Love leaves nothing whole or healthy in the man. It hinders his sleep so that he cannot rest either night or day. It takes away his appetite so that he has no desire for food or drink because of the continual torments of his heart and mind. It deprives him of all foresight <note>original: "providence"</note>; consequently, he neglects his affairs, his vocation, and his business. He cares for neither labor, study, nor prayer, casting away all thoughts of anything except the beloved body. This is his only study; this is his most vain occupation.

<summary>The author describes "inordinate love" as a total systemic infection of the body and soul that overrides reason, faith, and social responsibility. He argues that this passion acts as a destructive fire, blinding the individual to spiritual consequences and worldly duties.</summary>
<keywords>inordinate love, passion, reason vs. emotion, spiritual health, vocation, medical metaphor, moral philosophy</keywords>

# Basil Valentine’s Triumphant Chariot of Antimony

If the success for lovers is not equal to their wishes, or does not happen as quickly and prosperously as they desire, how many forms of melancholy arise! They suffer from such grief and sadness that they pine away and grow so lean that they have scarcely any flesh clinging to their bones. Indeed, at length they lose life itself, as can be proven by many examples. For such men—which is a horrible thing to think of—slight and neglect all perils and harms, both to the body and life, and even to the soul and eternal salvation.

But enough of these things; for it does not become a religious man <note>Basil Valentine was traditionally identified as a Benedictine monk.</note> to dwell too long upon these thoughts, or to give place to such a flame in his heart. Hitherto (I speak this without boasting) I have kept myself safe and free from it throughout the whole course of my life. I pray and invoke **GOD** to grant me his grace, so that I may keep holy and unbroken the faith which I have sworn, and live contented with my spiritual spouse, the Holy Catholic Church. I have mentioned these examples for no other reason than to express the love with which all tinctures <note>term: Tinctures; in alchemy, these are potent substances or "elixirs" capable of transforming and perfecting base metals.</note> ought to be moved toward metals, if they are ever to be admitted by them into true friendship, and be converted into a better state by a love that penetrates the innermost parts.

Now let us proceed to the preparation of the Stone <note>term: The Philosopher's Stone; the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life.</note>, and leave the discussion of its use to the end of this discourse. This Stone is of a penetrable and fiery nature, and is cooked and brought to maturity by fire. This is no different than all other things found in this world; however, as things are of a diverse nature, they obtain that maturity in different ways, according to how the nature of things provides different types of fires.

The first fire is **Celestial**, kindled by **GOD** in our...

### The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony

<page-num>153</page-num>

Our hearts, by which being inflamed, we are moved with love and a certain confidence in and of God our Creator, of the Most Holy and Incomprehensible *Trinity*, and of the mercy and grace of our Savior *JESUS CHRIST*. This confidence, kindled in us by love, never fails and never deserts us in our necessities, but will most certainly deliver our souls from everlasting destruction. 

The second fire is elemental, produced by the sun, and tends to the ripening of all things in the macrocosm <note>original: "Macrocosm"; the great world or the universe as a whole</note>. 

The third fire is corporal <note>physical or bodily</note>, with which all foods and medicines are cooked and prepared; without this, men can neither obtain health of body nor the sustenance of life. 

Of a fourth fire, mention is made in the Sacred Scriptures: namely, that which shall consume this visible world before the Supreme Judgment of God. But what that fire is, and how it shall operate, we must (if we be wise) leave to be judged by His own Supreme Majesty. 

A fifth fire is also spoken of in Holy Writ: namely, eternal fire. In this fire, which is never to have an end, the devils shall never be set at liberty from their infernal prison; and wicked men, their companions, being adjudged to those eternal fires, shall be vexed, punished, and miserably tormented forever. I pray the Omnipotent and merciful Lord to preserve us from this. Here I would admonish all and every creature endowed with reason to beg for that grace and mercy from the Omnipotent through their prayers, so that they may conform their lives to the divine precepts and their own duty, in order to escape this fire and its eternal torments.

Our "Stone of Fire" (which is to be noted) must be cooked and ripened with corporal fire in the microcosm <note>original: "Microcosm"; the "little world," referring here to the individual human being or the alchemist's laboratory vessel</note>, just as all other medicines and foods are prepared by the same. For where the great fire of...

<page-num>154</page-num>
# Basil Valentine's Triumphant Chariot of Antimony

Where the operation of the Macrocosm <note>The "Great World" or the universe at large</note> ceases, there the Microcosm <note>The "Little World," referring to the human body</note> begins to produce a new generation. Therefore, this concoction <note>original: "Concoction"; the process of "cooking" or refining a substance through heat</note> should seem strange to no man. Corn is increased and ripened by the elementary fire of the Macrocosm; but by the bodily fire of the Microcosm, a new cooking and maturation is achieved, so that man may use and enjoy that divine gift for his nourishment, and by the same process perfect the last and the least, which is produced from the first and the greatest.

The true Oil of *Antimony*, from which the aforementioned Stone of Fire is made, is sweet beyond measure. It is purged and separated from its earth in such a way that if a glass full of it is exposed to the sun, it casts forth various and wonderful rays—resembling a ruby and other colors—as if many fiery mirrors <note>original: "Speculums"</note> were present. Now pay attention, O lover of art and truth, and hear what I shall teach.

In the name of the Lord, take equal parts of the mineral <note>original: "Minera"</note> of *Antimony* (which grew after the rising of the sun) and saltpeter <note>original: "Salt Nitre"</note>. Grind them finely and mix them. Burn them together with a moderate fire, very skillfully and cautiously; for the principal part of the work consists in this. Then you will have a substance bordering on blackness. From this substance, make glass. Grind that glass to a fine powder and extract from it a deep red tincture using sharp distilled vinegar, which is made from its own mineral. Evaporate <note>original: "Abstract"</note> the vinegar in a water bath <note>original: "B. M." or Balneum Mariae</note>, and a powder will remain. Extract from this again with highly rectified spirit of wine <note>Concentrated alcohol</note>; then some dregs <note>original: "feces"</note> will be cast down, and you will have a beautiful, red, sweet extraction, which is of great use in medicine. This is the pure sulfur of *Antimony*, which must be separated as precisely as possible.

<summary>Basil Valentine explains how the human "Microcosm" completes the refinement of natural substances and provides a detailed alchemical recipe for extracting the "pure sulfur of Antimony" using saltpeter, heat, and distillation.</summary>

### Chariot of Antimony.

* Take heed, take heed, O Lover of Chemistry, lest by this fire you burn the wings of your bird, which has now raised itself to the top of the mountains. <note>This is an alchemical metaphor: the "bird" represents the volatile spirit or substance being refined; "burning the wings" means overheating it so it cannot be recovered or properly distilled.</note> Few words are sufficient to the intelligent; there is no need to repeat the same things often, as our careful father Basilius <note>Referring to Basil Valentine, the purported author of the text.</note> does that often enough.

If you have two ounces and two drachms of this extraction, take four drachms of the Salt of Antimony (prepared as I taught you), and pour the extraction over them. Circulate them for at least a whole month in a well-closed vessel, and the salt will unite itself with the extracted sulfur. If any dregs <note>original: "Faces"</note> settle at the bottom, separate them, and again draw off the spirit of wine using a water bath <note>original: "B. M." or Balneum Mariae.</note>. Drive the powder which remains with a strong fire, and—to your great wonder—there will come forth a multi-colored sweet oil, which is pleasing, transparent, and red. Rectify this oil again in a water bath so that a fourth part of it may be distilled, and then it is prepared.

<margin>* The term "Our Mercury," which has so often made you feel uncertain, is also to be understood here: for if you do not take the true Mercury of the Philosophers, you achieve nothing. Whoever candidly tells you this will be a Pylades to you, and you an Orestes to him <note>A reference to the Greek mythological figures who were the ultimate examples of loyal friendship.</note>, and nothing in life would be more pleasant to me than to join myself to you as a third companion in friendship.</margin>

* This operation being completed, take living <term>Mercury of Antimony</term> <note>A specialized substance the author claims to extract from antimony, distinct from common quicksilver.</note>, which I taught you how to make, and pour upon it red oil of vitriol <note>Concentrated sulfuric acid.</note> made upon iron and highly refined. By distillation in a sand bath, remove the watery moisture <note>original: "Phlegm"</note> from the mercury; then you will have a precious precipitate, in a color more pleasing to the sight than any other. In chronic diseases and open wounds, it may be profitably used for recovering original health. For it powerfully dries up all symptomatic humors from which "Martial" diseases <note>Diseases associated with the planet Mars, often involving inflammation, iron imbalances, or blood disorders.</note> proceed; in which the...

The spirit of the oil, which remains with the mercury <note>original: "Mercury"; here referring to the "mercury of antimony" or the metallic essence</note> and joins and unites itself to it, provides powerful assistance.

Take equal parts of this precipitate and of the superior sweet oil of antimony. Pour these together into a phial, which, once well-closed, should be set in a convenient heat. In time, the precipitate will dissolve and fix itself within the oil. Additionally, the phlegm <note>original: "Phlegm"; the watery, inactive byproduct of distillation</note> will be consumed by the fire, and what remains will become a red, dry, fixed, and fluid * powder, which will not emit any fumes at all.

<margin>* Stay far away, stay far away, you who are profane; and you who are initiated into the sacred mysteries of chemistry, keep silence. Let the King enter into his bedchamber, so that he may complete his marriage.</margin>

Now, my follower and disciple of secrets <note>original: "Arcanums"</note>, I will speak in a prophetic manner. When you have brought your philosophical studies to this end (following the method I have prescribed), you will possess the medicine of men and metals. It is pleasant and sweet to use, without any danger; it is penetrative, yet does not cause bowel movements <note>original: "Stools"</note>; it brings about improvement <note>original: "Emendation"</note> and expels evil. Use it as is appropriate, and it will yield you many benefits, both for health and for your worldly needs. By this means, you will be freed from want in this world, which is a matter of such great importance that no sacrifice of gratitude...

It will not readily cause harm; because it helps to recover original health and fights against venom, if any be in the body. It restores the vital spirits, cures "suffocation of the womb" <note>original: "Suffocation of the Matrix"; a historical term for hysterical or respiratory symptoms attributed to uterine displacement</note>, makes the menstrual flow begin if stopped, and stays it if it is excessive. It causes fruitfulness and makes the seed sound and effective for reproduction, both in women and men.

## Chariot of Antimony.

This will very clearly demonstrate the truth to you and open the way further, if you are a physician called by God to that office.

<margin>Here the medicinal virtues are spoken of at length. For God permits you not to be defiled with the filth of greed, but to be filled with the light of charity and burning with a desire to help your neighbor, following Him in discovering these secrets. Now farewell, O lover of chemistry; and if you have gained any light, either from the interpretation of Basil <note>original: "Basilius Valentinus," the purported 15th-century alchemist author</note> or my commentaries, enjoy it and communicate the same to the sons of art <note>A traditional term for practitioners of alchemy</note>. May philosophy, oppressed for so many years by the intolerable yoke of greed, at length be revived, and may there be a return to those times of the Egyptians, in which Hermes Trismegistus <note>The legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the Hermetic corpus is attributed</note> philosophized not with empty titles, but with wonderful works.</margin>

In these matters, I think I have done my part and written more than sufficiently concerning **Antimony**. If anyone follows me, he may add his own experiences to these, so that (with the singular favor of God) before the end of the world, the mysteries of the Most High may be revealed to His glory and honor, and for the preservation of health. Having finished this discourse, I intend to be silent for a time and return to my monastery, there to learn philosophy further so that I may be able to comment on other things. As I have already promised, I shall (God willing) write of **Vitriol** <note>Sulfuric acid or sulfate minerals</note>, common **Sulphur**, and the **Loadstone** <note>A naturally magnetized mineral; a magnet</note>, and reveal their **principles**, **powers**, **operations**, and **virtues**.

Let God, the Lord of Heaven and Earth, grant to us temporal health here, and hereafter eternal salvation for the refreshment of our souls in the seats of joy and gladness, never to be limited by any bounds of time. Amen.

Thus I conclude this **Treatise of Antimony**, and all that I have written concerning the **Red Oil of Antimony**, which is made of its highly purified **Sulphur**, and of the **Spirit**, which is prepared from its **Salt**. Incline...

# SYNESIUS
### TAKEN FROM THE
## Emperors' Library.

Although the ancient philosophers have written in various ways about this science, hiding the true principles of the Art <term>the "Great Work" of alchemy</term> under a multitude of names, they only did this for important reasons, as we will demonstrate later. And even though their expressions differ, they are not in any way inconsistent with one another; rather, they all aim toward a single goal and speak of the same thing. They thought it appropriate (above all else) to name the specific *Agent* <term>the active substance or catalyst that triggers a transformation</term> using a strange term—sometimes even one contrary to its actual nature and qualities.

Know then, my son, that almighty God, along with this universe, created two *stones*—that is to say, the *White* and the *Red*—both of which are un-

<meta>catchword: der</meta>

<summary>Synesius begins his alchemical treatise by explaining that ancient philosophers used cryptic, diverse names to hide the true principles of their art, though they all sought the same end. He introduces the concept of two fundamental "stones," the White and the Red, created by God.</summary>
<keywords>Synesius, alchemy, Ancient Philosophers, White Stone, Red Stone, alchemical Agent, esoteric secrets</keywords>

<header>The True Book</header>
<meta>catchword: not</meta>

[un]der one and the same subject, and afterwards multiplied in such abundance that every one may take as much as he pleases of it. The matter of these stones is of such a kind that it seems to be a middle ground <term>original: "mean"</term> between *metal* and *mercury*; it is partly fixed <note>stable or non-volatile</note> and partly not fixed, otherwise it could not be a middle ground between *metals* and *mercury*. This matter is the instrument by which our desire is accomplished, if we only prepare it correctly. 

Hence it happens that those who spend their efforts in this Art without using the said *medium* <note>the intermediary substance or catalyst</note> lose their labor; but if they are acquainted with the *Medium*, they shall find all things feasible and successful. Know then that this *Medium*, being aerial in nature, is found among the celestial bodies. It is only there that the Masculine and Feminine Genders are found (properly speaking), having a constant, strong, fixed, and permanent virtue <note>power or efficacy</note>. Philosophers have expressed themselves concerning its essence only through similitudes <note>comparisons or allegories</note> and figures. 

They did this so that the science might not be discovered by the ignorant. If that were to happen, everything would be lost. Instead, they intended it to be understood only by those patient souls and refined <term>original: "subtilized"</term> understandings which, being separated from the impurity <term>original: "soyliness"</term> of this world, are cleansed from the filth of that earthly dunghill of avarice. It is through greed that the ignorant are chained to the earthiness of this world which—without this admirable quintessence <note>the "fifth essence" or purest part of a substance</note>—is the receptacle of poverty. 

It is certain that those divine souls, once they have dived into *Democritus's Fountain* <note>Democritus was a Greek philosopher; his "fountain" refers to the source of natural truth and the metaphorical "well" from which wisdom is drawn</note> (that is to say, into the truth of Nature), would soon discover what confusion might happen in all social ranks <term>original: "estates"</term> and conditions if everyone could make as much gold as he wanted. For this reason, they were pleased to speak through figures, types, and analogies, so that they might not...

He argues that philosophers use metaphors to hide these secrets from the greedy, as the ability for anyone to create gold would destroy social and economic order.</summary>

...not be understood except by those who are discreet, religious, and enlightened by (divine) wisdom. Despite all this, they [the ancient philosophers] have left in their writings a certain method, way, and rule. With the assistance of this rule, a wise person may comprehend whatever they have written most obscurely and, in time, arrive at the knowledge of it—even if it means wading through some error first, as I have done, praised be God for it. And whereas the common <term>original: "Vulgar"</term> ignorant person ought to submit to these reasons and consequently adore what is too great to enter his brain, he instead accuses the philosophers of imposture and impiety. Because of this, and the scarcity of wise men, the Art <note>the "Great Work" of alchemy, or the pursuit of the Philosopher's Stone</note> falls into contempt.

But for my part, I tell you, they have always expressed themselves according to certain Truth, though very obscurely and sometimes through fables <note>original: "fabulously"</note>. I have deciphered all of this in this little treatise, and in such a way that the earnest seeker of science <note>In this context, science refers to systematized knowledge or "the secret art"</note> shall understand what has been mystically delivered by the philosophers. And yet, if he pretends to understand me but does not know the nature of the elements and created things, as well as our rich metal, he only loses his labor. But if he understands the harmony <term>original: "Concord"</term> and discord of natures, he will, by God’s assistance, arrive at the rest. Therefore, it is my prayer <note>original: "suit"</note> to God that whoever understands this present secret may work to the glory and praise of the sacred Divinity.

Know then, my dear son, that the ignorant man cannot comprehend the secret of the Art because it depends upon the knowledge of the true Body, which is hidden from him. Know then, my son, the pure and impure, the clean and unclean natures; for nothing can produce that which it does not already possess. Things that do not exist, or do not have certain qualities, can only give of their own nature. Make use then of

# The True Book

You will find that which is most perfect and nearest in kind, and it shall be enough. Avoid everything that is mixed and take only the *simple* <note>original: "simple"; in alchemy, a pure substance or a single herb/metal not combined with others</note>, for that comes from the *Quintessence* <term>the "fifth element," or the purest, most concentrated essence of a substance</term>. Note that we have two bodies of very great perfection, full of *Mercury*: extract your *Mercury* from these, and from that you shall make the *Medicine*—called by some the *Quintessence*—which is a power <note>original: "Vertue"</note> that is imperishable, permanent, and forever victorious. Indeed, it is a clear light that pours true goodness into every soul that has once tasted it. It is the knot and link of all the elements, which it contains within itself, being also the spirit that nourishes all things and by whose assistance Nature works in the universe.

It is the force, the beginning, and the end of the whole work. To be completely open with you, know that the *Quintessence* and the hidden thing of our Stone is nothing other than our viscous, celestial, and glorious soul, drawn by our mastery <note>original: "Magistery"; the alchemical process or the secret method used by a master</note> out of its mine, which generates itself. It is not possible for us to make that water by Art <note>human skill or technology</note>; Nature alone produces it. That water is the *most sharp vinegar*, which turns gold into a pure spirit. Indeed, it is that *blessed nature* which generates all things; through its decomposition <note>original: "putrefaction"</note>, it has become a tri-unity <note>a three-in-one substance, often referring to the union of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note>, and because of its greenness <note>original: "Viridity"</note>, it causes an appearance of various colors.

And I advise you, my son, to place no value on any other things (as they are useless); work only for that water which burns to blackness, whitens, dissolves, and thickens <note>original: "congeals"</note>. It is that which decomposes and causes growth <note>original: "germination"</note>; therefore, I advise you to devote yourself entirely to the boiling <note>original: "decoction"</note> of this water. Do not complain about the time it takes; otherwise, you will gain no advantage. Heat it gently, bit by bit, until it has changed its false color into a perfect one, and take great care at

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the alchemical process, focusing on the preservation of the "vitality" of the substance and explaining that the many different names for alchemical operations (like dissolving, washing, and grinding) all refer to the single process of refining nature toward perfection.</meta>

<page-num>167</page-num>
# Concerning the Philosopher's Stone

at the beginning, ensure that you do not burn its flowers <note>original: "Flowers"; in alchemy, the "flowers" refer to the sublimated essence or the finest part of a substance</note> or its vitality, and do not hurry too much to finish your work. Seal your vessel well so that what is inside cannot escape <note>original: "breath out"</note>, and in this way, you may bring it to completion. Note that to dissolve, to calcine <term>to reduce a substance to powder by heat</term>, to tinge, to whiten, to renew, to bathe, to wash, to coagulate, to imbibe, to decoct <term>to extract essence by boiling or heating</term>, to fix, to grind, to dry, and to distill, are all one thing; they signify nothing more than to mature <note>original: "concoct"</note> Nature until it is perfect.

Note further that to extract the soul <note>OCR "foul": a common transcription error for "soul" due to the archaic long "s"</note>, or the spirit, or the body, is nothing other than the aforementioned calcinations, as they represent the operation of Venus <note>In alchemy, Venus represents copper or a specific stage of refinement associated with the color green or harmony</note>. It is, therefore, through the fire used to extract the soul that the spirit comes forth gently—understand me well. The same may also be said of extracting the soul out of the body and subsequently returning it to that same body, until the whole is drawn into a mixture of all the four elements <term>earth, air, fire, and water</term>. 

In this way, that which is below is like that which is above <note>A famous alchemical maxim from the "Emerald Tablet," expressing the correspondence between the earthly work and the heavens</note>. Consequently, two luminaries <note>the sun and moon, representing gold and silver, or the active and passive principles</note> are created within: one is fixed and the other is not. The fixed remains below and the volatile remains above, moving perpetually until that which is below (the male) overcomes the female, and everything becomes fixed. Then, an incomparable Luminary emerges.

And as in the beginning there was only one, so in this matter, all proceeds from one and returns to one. This is called a "conversion of the elements." To convert the elements means to make the humid dry and the volatile <term>a substance that easily evaporates</term> fixed <term>solidified or made resistant to heat</term>, so that what is thick may be made thin. This weakens the part that fixes the rest, while the fixing part of the substance remains whole. Thus occurs the life and death of the elements, which, when combined, germinate and produce. So, one thing perfects another and helps it to withstand the fire.
